[CODE] (turn 30):
def construct_grid(N=60):
    total = N*N
    # compute degree for each cell
    deg = np.zeros((N,N), dtype=int)
    for i in range(N):
        for j in range(N):
            d=0
            if i>0: d+=1
            if i<N-1: d+=1
            if j>0: d+=1
            if j<N-1: d+=1
            deg[i,j]=d
    # separate cells into black and white based on parity
    black_mask = ((np.indices((N,N)).sum(axis=0) % 2) == 0)
    white_mask = ~black_mask
    # lists of cell indices for each color, sorted by degree (black ascending, white descending)
    black_cells = [(i,j) for i in range(N) for j in range(N) if black_mask[i,j]]
    white_cells = [(i,j) for i in range(N) for j in range(N) if white_mask[i,j]]
    black_cells.sort(key=lambda c: deg[c])  # ascending degree
    white_cells.sort(key=lambda c: -deg[c]) # descending degree
    # assign numbers
    grid = np.empty((N,N), dtype=int)
    # assign smallest numbers to black cells
    low_numbers = list(range(1, total//2 + 1))
    high_numbers = list(range(total//2 + 1, total + 1))
    # assign low numbers to black cells in order
    for cell, num in zip(black_cells, low_numbers):
        grid[cell] = num
    # assign high numbers to white cells in order
    for cell, num in zip(white_cells, high_numbers):
        grid[cell] = num
    return grid

grid60 = construct_grid(60)
min_sum = min_adj_sum_grid(grid60)
min_sum

def construct_grid(N=60):
    total = N*N
    # compute degree for each cell
    deg = np.zeros((N,N), dtype=int)
    for i in range(N):
        for j in range(N):
            d=0
            if i>0: d+=1
            if i<N-1: d+=1
            if j>0: d+=1
            if j<N-1: d+=1
            deg[i,j]=d
    # separate cells into black and white based on parity
    black_mask = ((np.indices((N,N)).sum(axis=0) % 2) == 0)
    white_mask = ~black_mask
    # lists of cell indices for each color, sorted by degree (black ascending, white descending)
    black_cells = [(i,j) for i in range(N) for j in range(N) if black_mask[i,j]]
    white_cells = [(i,j) for i in range(N) for j in range(N) if white_mask[i,j]]
    black_cells.sort(key=lambda c: deg[c])  # ascending degree
    white_cells.sort(key=lambda c: -deg[c]) # descending degree
    # assign numbers
    grid = np.empty((N,N), dtype=int)
    # assign smallest numbers to black cells
    low_numbers = list(range(1, total//2 + 1))
    high_numbers = list(range(total//2 + 1, total + 1))
    # assign low numbers to black cells in order
    for cell, num in zip(black_cells, low_numbers):
        grid[cell] = num
    # assign high numbers to white cells in order
    for cell, num in zip(white_cells, high_numbers):
        grid[cell] = num
    return grid

def find_min_edge(grid):
    N = grid.shape[0]
    min_val = 10**9
    min_pair = None
    for i in range(N):
        for j in range(N):
            if i+1<N:
                s = grid[i,j] + grid[i+1,j]
                if s < min_val:
                    min_val = s
                    min_pair = ((i,j),(i+1,j))
            if j+1<N:
                s = grid[i,j] + grid[i,j+1]
                if s < min_val:
                    min_val = s
                    min_pair = ((i,j),(i,j+1))
    return min_val, min_pair

min_val, min_pair = find_min_edge(grid60)
min_val, min_pair

def max_S_grid(m, n):
    V = m*n
    # map cell index
    def cell_index(i,j):
        return i*n + j
    # edges list (undirected)
    edges = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            u = cell_index(i,j)
            if i+1 < m:
                v = cell_index(i+1,j)
                edges.append((u,v))
            if j+1 < n:
                v = cell_index(i,j+1)
                edges.append((u,v))
    # number of variables: V*V binary + 1 integer S
    num_vars = V*V + 1
    # bounds: binary for first V*V, integer for S
    # We'll set bounds for binary as (0,1)
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    # S bounds
    lb[-1] = 0
    ub[-1] = 2*V-1
    # integrality: 1 for binary, 1 for S (integer)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # constraints
    A_eq_rows = []
    b_eq = []
    # vertex assignment constraints
    for v in range(V):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            idx = v*V + (k-1)
            row[idx] = 1
        A_eq_rows.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # number assignment constraints
    for k in range(1, V+1):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for v in range(V):
            idx = v*V + (k-1)
            row[idx] = 1
        A_eq_rows.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # edge constraints: -sum k x_u,k - sum l x_v,l + S <= 0
    A_ub_rows = []
    b_ub = []
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            idx_u = u*V + (k-1)
            idx_v = v*V + (k-1)
            row[idx_u] -= k
            row[idx_v] -= k
        row[-1] = 1  # coefficient for S
        A_ub_rows.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    # Convert to numpy arrays
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq_rows)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub = np.array(A_ub_rows)
    b_ub = np.array(b_ub)
    # Objective: maximize S -> minimize -S
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1  # minimize -S
    # Solve MILP
    res = milp(c=c, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub,
               integrality=integrality, bounds=Bounds(lb, ub), options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed")
        return None
    S_opt = int(round(res.x[-1]))
    # Could also extract assignment
    return S_opt, res.x

print(max_S_grid(2,2))

def max_S_grid(m, n):
    V = m*n
    # map cell index
    def cell_index(i,j):
        return i*n + j
    # edges list (undirected)
    edges = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            u = cell_index(i,j)
            if i+1 < m:
                v = cell_index(i+1,j)
                edges.append((u,v))
            if j+1 < n:
                v = cell_index(i,j+1)
                edges.append((u,v))
    # number of variables: V*V binary + 1 integer S
    num_vars = V*V + 1
    # bounds: binary for first V*V, integer for S
    # We'll set bounds for binary as (0,1)

    # S bounds
    lb[-1] = 0
    ub[-1] = 2*V-1
    # integrality: 1 for binary, 1 for S (integer)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # constraints
    A_eq_rows = []
    b_eq = []
    # vertex assignment constraints
    for v in range(V):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            idx = v*V + (k-1)
            row[idx] = 1
        A_eq_rows.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # number assignment constraints
    for k in range(1, V+1):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for v in range(V):
            idx = v*V + (k-1)
            row[idx] = 1
        A_eq_rows.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # edge constraints: -sum k x_u,k - sum l x_v,l + S <= 0
    A_ub_rows = []
    b_ub = []
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            idx_u = u*V + (k-1)
            idx_v = v*V + (k-1)
            row[idx_u] -= k
            row[idx_v] -= k
        row[-1] = 1  # coefficient for S
        A_ub_rows.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    # Convert to numpy arrays
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq_rows)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub = np.array(A_ub_rows)
    b_ub = np.array(b_ub)
    # Objective: maximize S -> minimize -S
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1  # minimize -S
    # Solve MILP
    res = milp(c=c, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub,
               integrality=integrality, bounds=Bounds(lb, ub), options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed")
        return None
    S_opt = int(round(res.x[-1]))
    # Could also extract assignment
    return S_opt, res.x

def milp(c, *, integrality=None, bounds=None, constraints=None, options=None):
    r"""
    Mixed-integer linear programming

    Solves problems of the following form:

    .. math::

        \min_x \ & c^T x \\
        \mbox{such that} \ & b_l \leq A x \leq b_u,\\
        & l \leq x \leq u, \\
        & x_i \in \mathbb{Z}, i \in X_i

    where :math:`x` is a vector of decision variables;
    :math:`c`, :math:`b_l`, :math:`b_u`, :math:`l`, and :math:`u` are vectors;
    :math:`A` is a matrix, and :math:`X_i` is the set of indices of
    decision variables that must be integral. (In this context, a
    variable that can assume only integer values is said to be "integral";
    it has an "integrality" constraint.)

    Alternatively, that's:

    minimize::

        c @ x

    such that::

        b_l <= A @ x <= b_u
        l <= x <= u
        Specified elements of x must be integers

    By default, ``l = 0`` and ``u = np.inf`` unless specified with
    ``bounds``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    c : 1D dense array_like
        The coefficients of the linear objective function to be minimized.
        `c` is converted to a double precision array before the problem is
        solved.
    integrality : 1D dense array_like, optional
        Indicates the type of integrality constraint on each decision variable.

        ``0`` : Continuous variable; no integrality constraint.

        ``1`` : Integer variable; decision variable must be an integer
        within `bounds`.

        ``2`` : Semi-continuous variable; decision variable must be within
... (200 lines total)

[CODE] (turn 7):
def max_S_grid_milp(m, n):
    V = m*n
    # cell index
    def idx(i,j):
        return i*n + j
    edges = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            u = idx(i,j)
            if i+1 < m:
                edges.append((u, idx(i+1,j)))
            if j+1 < n:
                edges.append((u, idx(i,j+1)))
    num_vars = V*V + 1  # binary assignment + S
    # Build constraints
    rows = []
    b_l = []
    b_u = []
    # vertex assignment equality
    for v in range(V):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            row[v*V + (k-1)] = 1
        rows.append(row)
        b_l.append(1)
        b_u.append(1)
    # number assignment equality
    for k in range(1, V+1):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for v in range(V):
            row[v*V + (k-1)] = 1
        rows.append(row)
        b_l.append(1)
        b_u.append(1)
    # edge constraints: -sum k x_u,k - sum k x_v,k + S <= 0
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            row[u*V + (k-1)] -= k
            row[v*V + (k-1)] -= k
        row[-1] = 1  # S coefficient
        rows.append(row)
        b_l.append(-np.inf)
        b_u.append(0)
    A = np.vstack(rows)
    b_l = np.array(b_l, dtype=float)
    b_u = np.array(b_u, dtype=float)
    # bounds
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    # S bounds
    lb[-1] = 0
    ub[-1] = 2*V-1
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    # integrality: all variables integer (binary for first part, integer for S)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # objective: maximize S => minimize -S
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    constraints = LinearConstraint(A, b_l, b_u)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed")
        return None
    S_opt = int(round(res.x[-1]))
    return S_opt, res.x

print(max_S_grid_milp(2,2)[0])

import numpy as np, math, itertools, random, collections, functools, fractions, decimal, heapq, statistics, sys, math, numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_S_grid_milp(m, n):
    V = m*n
    def idx(i,j):
        return i*n + j
    edges = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            u = idx(i,j)
            if i+1 < m:
                edges.append((u, idx(i+1,j)))
            if j+1 < n:
                edges.append((u, idx(i,j+1)))
    num_vars = V*V + 1
    rows = []
    b_l = []
    b_u = []
    # vertex assignment
    for v in range(V):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            row[v*V + (k-1)] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(1); b_u.append(1)
    # number assignment
    for k in range(1, V+1):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for v in range(V):
            row[v*V + (k-1)] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(1); b_u.append(1)
    # edge constraints
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            row[u*V + (k-1)] -= k
            row[v*V + (k-1)] -= k
        row[-1] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(-np.inf); b_u.append(0)
    A = np.vstack(rows)
    b_l = np.array(b_l, dtype=float)
    b_u = np.array(b_u, dtype=float)
    # bounds
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    lb[-1] = 0
    ub[-1] = 2*V-1
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    constraints = LinearConstraint(A, b_l, b_u)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed")
        return None
    S_opt = int(round(res.x[-1]))
    return S_opt, res.x

def max_S_grid_milp(m, n):

    def idx(i,j):
        return i*n + j

def max_S_grid_milp(m, n):
    V = m*n
    def idx(i,j):
        return i*n + j
    edges = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):

            if i+1 < m:
                edges.append((u, idx(i+1,j)))
            if j+1 < n:
                edges.append((u, idx(i,j+1)))
    num_vars = V*V + 1
    rows = []
    b_l = []
    b_u = []
    # vertex assignment
    for v in range(V):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            row[v*V + (k-1)] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(1); b_u.append(1)
    # number assignment
    for k in range(1, V+1):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for v in range(V):
            row[v*V + (k-1)] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(1); b_u.append(1)
    # edge constraints
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            row[u*V + (k-1)] -= k
            row[v*V + (k-1)] -= k
        row[-1] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(-np.inf); b_u.append(0)
    A = np.vstack(rows)
    b_l = np.array(b_l, dtype=float)
    b_u = np.array(b_u, dtype=float)
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    lb[-1] = 0
    ub[-1] = 2*V-1
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    constraints = LinearConstraint(A, b_l, b_u)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed")
        return None
    S_opt = int(round(res.x[-1]))
    return S_opt, res.x

print(max_S_grid_milp(2,8)[0])

[CODE] (turn 24):
def max_S_grid_milp(m, n):
    V = m*n
    def idx(i,j):
        return i*n + j
    edges = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            u = idx(i,j)
            if i+1 < m:
                edges.append((u, idx(i+1,j)))
            if j+1 < n:
                edges.append((u, idx(i,j+1)))
    num_vars = V*V + 1
    rows = []
    b_l = []
    b_u = []
    # vertex assignment
    for v in range(V):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            row[v*V + (k-1)] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(1); b_u.append(1)
    # number assignment
    for k in range(1, V+1):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for v in range(V):
            row[v*V + (k-1)] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(1); b_u.append(1)
    # edge constraints
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1, V+1):
            row[u*V + (k-1)] -= k
            row[v*V + (k-1)] -= k
        row[-1] = 1
        rows.append(row); b_l.append(-np.inf); b_u.append(0)
    A = np.vstack(rows)
    b_l = np.array(b_l, dtype=float)
    b_u = np.array(b_u, dtype=float)
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    lb[-1] = 0
    ub[-1] = 2*V-1
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    constraints = LinearConstraint(A, b_l, b_u)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed")
        return None
    S_opt = int(round(res.x[-1]))
    return S_opt, res.x

print(max_S_grid_milp(2,8)[0])

[CODE] (turn 1):
import itertools, math

def max_min_sum(m,n):
    N=m*n
    # cells indexed 0..N-1, adjacency defined by grid
    # generate all permutations of numbers 1..N (small only)
    best=-1
    best_perm=None
    # adjacency list
    adj=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            neigh=[]
            for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
                ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
                if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                    neigh.append(ni*n+nj)
            adj.append(neigh)
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        # compute min sum across edges (each edge counted once)
        min_sum=math.inf
        visited=set()
        for u in range(N):
            for v in adj[u]:
                if (v,u) in visited: continue
                visited.add((u,v))
                s=perm[u]+perm[v]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
                    if min_sum<=best: # prune
                        break
            else:
                continue
            break
        else:
            # all edges checked
            if min_sum>best:
                best=min_sum
                best_perm=perm
    return best, best_perm

for m,n in [(2,2),(2,3),(3,3)]:
    print(m,n, max_min_sum(m,n)[0])

[CODE] (turn 3):
import random, math, itertools, time, sys, collections, itertools, numpy as np, math, sys, random, time, itertools, collections, math, numpy as np
def min_edge_sum(grid, m,n):
    # grid is list of numbers length m*n
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
                ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
                if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                    nid=ni*n+nj
                    if nid>idx: # count each edge once
                        s=grid[idx]+grid[nid]
                        if s<min_sum:
                            min_sum=s
    return min_sum

def random_search(m,n,iterations=200000):
    N=m*n
    best=0
    best_grid=None
    for _ in range(iterations):
        perm=list(range(1,N+1))
        random.shuffle(perm)
        s=min_edge_sum(perm,m,n)
        if s>best:
            best=s
            best_grid=perm[:]
    return best,best_grid

print(random_search(3,4,50000))

import itertools, math, numpy as np, sys, time, random, collections, math, numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def feasible_S(m,n,S):
    N=m*n
    # variables: x_{i,k} for i in 0..N-1, k in 1..N
    # flatten index: i*N + (k-1)
    num_vars = N*N
    # constraints:
    # 1) each cell assigned exactly one number
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[i*N + (k-1)] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # 2) each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(1,N+1):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for i in range(N):
            row[i*N + (k-1)] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq)
    # inequality constraints for edges: sum_i k*x_i_k + sum_j l*x_j_l >= S
    # Convert to -sum >= -S -> A_ub * x <= b_ub where A_ub = -coeffs, b_ub = -S
    edges=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]: # only right and down to avoid duplicates
                ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
                if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                    nid=ni*n+nj
                    edges.append((idx,nid))
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[u*N + (k-1)] = -k
            row[v*N + (k-1)] = -k
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(-S)
    A_ub = np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub = np.array(b_ub)
    # variable bounds 0 <= x <= 1, integer
    bounds = Bounds(0,1)
    # integrality vector
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # objective: dummy zero vector (feasibility)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq),
                                 LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf*np.ones(len(b_ub)), b_ub)],
                bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    return res.success

def max_S_bruteforce(m,n):
    N=m*n
    lo=0
    hi=2*N+1 # max possible sum is (N)+(N) = 2N
    while lo+1<hi:
        mid=(lo+hi)//2
        print("checking",mid)
        if feasible_S(m,n,mid):
            lo=mid
        else:
            hi=mid
    return lo

print("Start")

import numpy as np, math, itertools, random, sys, time
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def feasible_S(m,n,S):
    N=m*n
    num_vars=N*N
    # Build constraints
    # Equality constraints for each cell and each number
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    for i in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[i*N + (k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    for k in range(1,N+1):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        for i in range(N):
            row[i*N + (k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # Edge constraints
    edges=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
                ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
                if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                    nid=ni*n+nj
                    edges.append((idx,nid))
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[u*N + (k-1)] = -k
            row[v*N + (k-1)] = -k
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(-S)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    bounds=Bounds(0,1)
    integrality=np.ones(num_vars,dtype=int)
    c=np.zeros(num_vars)
    # Build constraints list
    constraints=[LinearConstraint(A_eq,b_eq,b_eq)]
    if len(A_ub)>0:
        constraints.append(LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf*np.ones(len(b_ub)),b_ub))
    res=milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve':True,'disp':False})
    return res.success

print(feasible_S(3,4,12))

import numpy as np, math, itertools, random, sys, time
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def feasible_S(m,n,S):
    N=m*n
    num_vars=N*N
    # Build constraints
    # Equality constraints for each cell and each number
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    for i in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[i*N + (k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    for k in range(1,N+1):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        for i in range(N):
            row[i*N + (k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # Edge constraints
    edges=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
                ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
                if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                    nid=ni*n+nj
                    edges.append((idx,nid))
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[u*N + (k-1)] = -k
            row[v*N + (k-1)] = -k
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(-S)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    bounds=Bounds(0,1)
    integrality=np.ones(num_vars,dtype=int)
    c=np.zeros(num_vars)
    # Build constraints list
    constraints=[LinearConstraint(A_eq,b_eq,b_eq)]
    if len(A_ub)>0:
        constraints.append(LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf*np.ones(len(b_ub)),b_ub))
    res=milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve':True,'disp':False})
    return res.success

def feasible_S(m,n,S):

def feasible_S_small(m,n,S):
    N=m*n

import numpy as np, math, itertools, random, sys, time
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def feasible_S(m,n,S):
    N=m*n
    num_vars=N*N
    # Build equality constraints for each cell and each number
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # cell constraints
    for i in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        row[i*N:(i+1)*N]=1  # all numbers for cell i
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # number constraints
    for k in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        row[k::N]=1  # all cells for number k+1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # Edge constraints
    edges=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
                ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
                if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                    nid=ni*n+nj
                    edges.append((idx,nid))
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        # for each possible number k (1-indexed)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[u*N + (k-1)] = -k
            row[v*N + (k-1)] = -k
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(-S)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    bounds=Bounds(0,1)
    integrality=np.ones(num_vars,dtype=int)
    c=np.zeros(num_vars)
    constraints=[LinearConstraint(A_eq,b_eq,b_eq)]
    if len(A_ub)>0:
        constraints.append(LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf*np.ones(len(b_ub)),b_ub))
    res=milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve':True,'disp':False})
    return res.success

print(feasible_S(4,5,20))

    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)

[CODE] (turn 24):
import random, math, time, itertools, collections, numpy as np

def min_edge_sum_grid(grid,m,n):
    N=m*n
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
                ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
                if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                    nid=ni*n+nj
                    s=grid[idx]+grid[nid]
                    if s<min_sum:
                        min_sum=s
    return min_sum

def random_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    perm=list(range(1,N+1))
    random.shuffle(perm)
    return perm

def hill_climb(m,n,iterations=20000):
    N=m*n
    # start with random
    grid=random_grid(m,n)
    best=min_edge_sum_grid(grid,m,n)
    best_grid=grid[:]
    for it in range(iterations):
        # propose swap two cells
        i,j=random.sample(range(N),2)
        grid[i],grid[j]=grid[j],grid[i]
        cur=min_edge_sum_grid(grid,m,n)
        if cur>=best:
            best=cur
            best_grid=grid[:]
        else:
            # revert with probability maybe
            # simple hill climb: revert
            grid[i],grid[j]=grid[j],grid[i]
    return best,best_grid

best,bg=hill_climb(5,5,iterations=200000)
best

import random, math, time, itertools, collections, numpy as np

def min_edge_sum_grid(grid,m,n):

    min_sum=10**9

def random_grid(m,n):

    perm=list(range(1,N+1))
    random.shuffle(perm)
    return perm

def hill_climb(m,n,iterations=20000):

    # start with random
    grid=random_grid(m,n)
    best=min_edge_sum_grid(grid,m,n)
    best_grid=grid[:]
    for it in range(iterations):

        i,j=random.sample(range(N),2)
        grid[i],grid[j]=grid[j],grid[i]
        cur=min_edge_sum_grid(grid,m,n)

            best=cur
            best_grid=grid[:]

            # revert with probability maybe
            # simple hill climb: revert
            grid[i],grid[j]=grid[j],grid[i]

import math
def weighted_sum():
    # corners
    sum_corners = sum(range(1,5))
    # edges non-corner
    sum_edges = sum(range(5,237))
    # interior
    sum_interior = sum(range(237,3601))
    total = 2*sum_corners + 3*sum_edges + 4*sum_interior
    return total
total = weighted_sum()
total

    # interior

    return total

[CODE] (turn 6):
def max_min_adjacent_sum(m, n):
    N = m*n
    # Map cell index to (r,c)
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    cell_index = { (r,c): i for i,(r,c) in enumerate(cells) }
    # edges list of pairs of cell indices
    edges = []
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i = cell_index[(r,c)]
            if r+1 < m:
                j = cell_index[(r+1,c)]
                edges.append((i,j))
            if c+1 < n:
                j = cell_index[(r,c+1)]
                edges.append((i,j))
    # Variables: y_{i,k} binary for each cell i and number k (1..N)
    # We'll flatten to vector length N*N
    num_vars = N*N + 1  # plus S variable at end
    # Build constraints
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    # each cell assigned exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for i in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # Edge constraints: sum_{k} k*y_{i,k} + sum_{l} l*y_{j,l} >= S
    # We'll rewrite as -sum_i - sum_j + S <= 0 (linear inequality)
    A_ub = []
    b_ub = []
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        # coefficients for y_{i,k}
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = - (k+1)  # negative because moving to LHS
        for k in range(N):
            row[j*N + k] = - (k+1)
        # coefficient for S variable (last index)
        row[-1] = 1  # + S
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    # variable bounds: y binary 0/1, S integer variable with bounds 0..2N maybe
    # We'll set bounds for all variables
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    # For S variable, set ub large
    ub[-1] = 2*N  # max possible sum
    # integer constraints: all variables integer
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # Build LinearConstraint objects
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub = np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub = np.array(b_ub)
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ub = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)  # Actually we need A_ub x <= b_ub
    # Wait we used A_ub row * x <= b_ub (0). So we can pass as LinearConstraint with ub=b_ub, lb=-inf
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    # objective: maximize S => minimize -S
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1  # minimize -S
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed", res.message)
        return None
    S_opt = int(round(res.x[-1]))
    return S_opt

[CODE] (turn 6):
def max_min_adjacent_sum(m, n):
    N = m*n
    # Map cell index to (r,c)
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    cell_index = { (r,c): i for i,(r,c) in enumerate(cells) }
    # edges list of pairs of cell indices
    edges = []
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i = cell_index[(r,c)]
            if r+1 < m:
                j = cell_index[(r+1,c)]
                edges.append((i,j))
            if c+1 < n:
                j = cell_index[(r,c+1)]
                edges.append((i,j))
    # Variables: y_{i,k} binary for each cell i and number k (1..N)
    # We'll flatten to vector length N*N
    num_vars = N*N + 1  # plus S variable at end
    # Build constraints
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    # each cell assigned exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for i in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # Edge constraints: sum_{k} k*y_{i,k} + sum_{l} l*y_{j,l} >= S
    # We'll rewrite as -sum_i - sum_j + S <= 0 (linear inequality)
    A_ub = []
    b_ub = []
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        # coefficients for y_{i,k}
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = - (k+1)  # negative because moving to LHS
        for k in range(N):
            row[j*N + k] = - (k+1)
        # coefficient for S variable (last index)
        row[-1] = 1  # + S
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    # variable bounds: y binary 0/1, S integer variable with bounds 0..2N maybe
    # We'll set bounds for all variables
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    # For S variable, set ub large
    ub[-1] = 2*N  # max possible sum
    # integer constraints: all variables integer
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # Build LinearConstraint objects
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub = np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub = np.array(b_ub)
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ub = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)  # Actually we need A_ub x <= b_ub
    # Wait we used A_ub row * x <= b_ub (0). So we can pass as LinearConstraint with ub=b_ub, lb=-inf
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    # objective: maximize S => minimize -S
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1  # minimize -S
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed", res.message)
        return None
    S_opt = int(round(res.x[-1]))
    return S_opt

[CODE] (turn 8):
import itertools, math
def brute_max_min(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = list(range(N))
    best=0
    bestperm=None
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        # compute min edge sum
        grid = [perm[i*n:(i+1)*n] for i in range(m)]
        mins=10**9
        for i in range(m):
            for j in range(n):
                if i+1<m:
                    mins=min(mins, grid[i][j]+grid[i+1][j])
                if j+1<n:
                    mins=min(mins, grid[i][j]+grid[i][j+1])
        if mins>best:
            best=mins
            bestperm=perm
    return best, bestperm
print(brute_max_min(2,2)[0])

[CODE] (turn 11):
def bound(m,n):
    N=m*n
    # compute degree distribution
    corners=4
    edge_noncorner=2*m+2*n-8  # perimeter cells minus corners
    interior=N - corners - edge_noncorner
    # assign numbers
    # smallest corners numbers 1..corners
    # next edge numbers corners+1 .. corners+edge_noncorner
    # interior numbers rest
    sum_corners=sum(range(1,corners+1))
    sum_edges=sum(range(corners+1, corners+edge_noncorner+1))
    sum_interior=sum(range(corners+edge_noncorner+1, N+1))
    total=2*sum_corners + 3*sum_edges + 4*sum_interior
    edges = (m-1)*n + m*(n-1)
    return total/edges
print(bound(3,3))

def bound(m,n):
    N=m*n
    # compute degree distribution
    corners=4
    edge_noncorner=2*m+2*n-8  # perimeter cells minus corners
    interior=N - corners - edge_noncorner
    # assign numbers
    # smallest corners numbers 1..corners
    # next edge numbers corners+1 .. corners+edge_noncorner
    # interior numbers rest
    sum_corners=sum(range(1,corners+1))
    sum_edges=sum(range(corners+1, corners+edge_noncorner+1))
    sum_interior=sum(range(corners+edge_noncorner+1, N+1))
    total=2*sum_corners + 3*sum_edges + 4*sum_interior
    edges = (m-1)*n + m*(n-1)
    return total/edges
print(bound(3,3))

def max_min_adjacent_sum_milp(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i=idx[(r,c)]
            if r+1<m:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
            if c+1<n:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    # variables: y[i,k] binary for i in 0..N-1, k in 0..N-1 (represent number k+1)
    # plus S variable at end
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    # Build constraints
    import numpy as np
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell assigned exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[i*N:(i+1)*N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[k::N] = 1  # every i where variable index i*N + k
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # edge constraints: -sum_i - sum_j + S <= 0
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        # subtract contributions
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
            row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
        row[-1] = 1  # +S
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1  # maximize S
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    ub[-1] = 2*N
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("fail", res.message)
        return None
    return int(round(res.x[-1]))
print(max_min_adjacent_sum_milp(3,4))

def max_min_adjacent_sum_milp(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i=idx[(r,c)]
            if r+1<m:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
            if c+1<n:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    # variables: y[i,k] binary for i in 0..N-1, k in 0..N-1 (represent number k+1)
    # plus S variable at end
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    # Build constraints
    import numpy as np
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell assigned exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[i*N:(i+1)*N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[k::N] = 1  # every i where variable index i*N + k
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # edge constraints: -sum_i - sum_j + S <= 0
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        # subtract contributions
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
            row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
        row[-1] = 1  # +S
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1  # maximize S
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    ub[-1] = 2*N
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("fail", res.message)
        return None
    return int(round(res.x[-1]))

def solve_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
for c in range(n):
i=idx[(r,c)]
if r+1<m:
    edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
if c+1<n:
    edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    import numpy as np
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
row[i*N:(i+1)*N] = 1
A_eq.append(row)
b_eq.append(1)
    # each number exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
row[k::N] = 1
A_eq.append(row)
b_eq.append(1)
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
for k in range(N):
row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
row[-1] = 1
A_ub.append(row)
b_ub.append(0)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    ub[-1] = 2*N
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    print("status", res.success, res.message)
    if res.success:
print("S", res.x[-1])
    return res

[CODE] (turn 24):
def solve_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i=idx[(r,c)]
            if r+1<m:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
            if c+1<n:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    import numpy as np
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[i*N:(i+1)*N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[k::N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
            row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
        row[-1] = 1
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    ub[-1] = 2*N
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    print("Success:", res.success, "msg:", res.message)
    if res.success:
        print("S:", res.x[-1])
    return res

solve_grid(2,2)

def solve_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i=idx[(r,c)]
            if r+1<m:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
            if c+1<n:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    import numpy as np
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[i*N:(i+1)*N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[k::N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
            row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
        row[-1] = 1
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    ub[-1] = 2*N
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve': True, 'disp': False})
    print("Success:", res.success, "msg:", res.message)
    if res.success:
        print("S:", res.x[-1])
    return res

[CODE] (turn 26):
def extract_assignment(res,m,n):
    N=m*n
    x=res.x
    assign = np.zeros(N, dtype=int)
    for i in range(N):
        for k in range(N):
            if round(x[i*N + k]) == 1:
                assign[i] = k+1
                break
    return assign.reshape((m,n))
assign = extract_assignment(res,2,2)
assign

[CODE] (turn 28):
def min_edge_sum(grid):
    m,n = grid.shape
    mins = 10**9
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1<m:
                mins = min(mins, grid[i,j] + grid[i+1,j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins = min(mins, grid[i,j] + grid[i,j+1])
    return mins

print(min_edge_sum(assign))

def compute_S_upper_bounds(grid):
    m,n = grid.shape
    ub = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1<m:
                ub.append(grid[i,j] + grid[i+1,j])
            if j+1<n:
                ub.append(grid[i,j] + grid[i,j+1])
    return min(ub)
print(compute_S_upper_bounds(assign))

                ub.append(grid[i,j] + grid[i+1,j])
            if j+1<n:
                ub.append(grid[i,j] + grid[i,j+1])
    return min(ub)

# Let's inspect first edge constraint row for 2x2 grid
def get_edge_rows(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i=idx[(r,c)]
            if r+1<m:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
            if c+1<n:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    import numpy as np
    rows=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
            row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
        row[-1] = 1
        rows.append(row)
    return rows, edges

rows, edges = get_edge_rows(2,2)
print(edges)
print(rows[0][:10], rows[0][-5:])

def get_edge_rows(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i=idx[(r,c)]
            if r+1<m:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
            if c+1<n:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    import numpy as np
    rows=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
            row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
        row[-1] = 1
        rows.append(row)
    return rows, edges

[CODE] (turn 31):
def assignment_vector(assign):
    m,n = assign.shape
    N=m*n
    vec = np.zeros(N*N)
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            idx = i*n + j
            val = assign[i,j]
            vec[idx*N + (val-1)] = 1
    return vec

vec = assignment_vector(assign)
# compute left-hand side for each edge row
lhs = [row @ np.concatenate([vec, [0]]) for row in rows]  # S=0 currently
lhs

[CODE] (turn 35):
import random, math, numpy as np, itertools, time

def min_edge_sum_grid(grid):
    m,n = grid.shape
    mins = 10**9
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1<m:
                mins = min(mins, grid[i,j] + grid[i+1,j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins = min(mins, grid[i,j] + grid[i,j+1])
    return mins

def random_grid(m,n):
    nums = list(range(1,m*n+1))
    random.shuffle(nums)
    return np.array(nums).reshape((m,n))

def improve_grid(grid, iterations=100000):
    m,n = grid.shape
    best = min_edge_sum_grid(grid)
    best_grid = grid.copy()
    for it in range(iterations):
        # pick two random cells to swap
        i1,j1 = random.randrange(m), random.randrange(n)
        i2,j2 = random.randrange(m), random.randrange(n)
        if i1==i2 and j1==j2:
            continue
        # swap
        grid[i1,j1], grid[i2,j2] = grid[i2,j2], grid[i1,j1]
        cur = min_edge_sum_grid(grid)
        if cur >= best:
            best = cur
            best_grid = grid.copy()
        else:
            # revert with probability maybe
            # simple hill climbing: revert if not better
            grid[i1,j1], grid[i2,j2] = grid[i2,j2], grid[i1,j1]
    return best, best_grid

def heuristic_max_min(m,n,tries=5,iterations=20000):
    best_overall = 0
    best_grid = None
    for t in range(tries):
        grid = random_grid(m,n)
        best, g = improve_grid(grid, iterations)
        if best > best_overall:
            best_overall = best
            best_grid = g
    return best_overall, best_grid

best5, grid5 = heuristic_max_min(5,5,tries=10,iterations=50000)
best5

def solve_grid_milp(m,n, time_limit=30):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i=idx[(r,c)]
            if r+1<m:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
            if c+1<n:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    import numpy as np
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[i*N:(i+1)*N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[k::N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
            row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
        row[-1] = 1
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    ub[-1] = 2*N
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # set options time limit
    options = {'presolve': True, 'disp': False, 'time_limit': time_limit}
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options=options)
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP not solved:", res.message)
        return None
    return int(round(res.x[-1]))

print(solve_grid_milp(4,4))

[CODE] (turn 38):
def solve_grid_milp(m,n, time_limit=30):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(r,c) for r in range(m) for c in range(n)]
    idx = {cell:i for i,cell in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for r in range(m):
        for c in range(n):
            i=idx[(r,c)]
            if r+1<m:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r+1,c)]))
            if c+1<n:
                edges.append((i, idx[(r,c+1)]))
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    import numpy as np
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[i*N:(i+1)*N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[k::N] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = -(k+1)
            row[j*N + k] = -(k+1)
        row[-1] = 1
        A_ub.append(row)
        b_ub.append(0)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
    b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    ub[-1] = 2*N
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # set options time limit
    options = {'presolve': True, 'disp': False, 'time_limit': time_limit}
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options=options)
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP not solved:", res.message)
        return None
    return int(round(res.x[-1]))

print(solve_grid_milp(4,4))

def solve_two_cells():
    N=2
    num_vars = N*N + 1
    import numpy as np
    # constraints: each cell assigned one number
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    # cell0
    row = np.zeros(num_vars, int)
    row[0:2] = 1
    A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    # cell1
    row = np.zeros(num_vars, int)
    row[2:4] = 1
    A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to one cell
    row = np.zeros(num_vars, int)
    row[0::2] = 1  # number1 assigned to cell0 or cell1
    A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    row = np.zeros(num_vars, int)
    row[1::2] = 1
    A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    # edge constraint: S - sum_i - sum_j <= 0
    row = np.zeros(num_vars, int)
    # sum_i = 1*y_i1 + 2*y_i2
    row[0] = -1
    row[1] = -2
    row[2] = -1
    row[3] = -2
    row[-1] = 1
    A_ub = np.array([row])
    b_ub = np.array([0])
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq)
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    lin_eq = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
    lin_ineq = LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    c[-1] = -1
    lb = np.zeros(num_vars)
    ub = np.ones(num_vars)
    ub[-1] = 2*N
    bounds = Bounds(lb, ub)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, int)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=[lin_eq, lin_ineq], bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'disp': False})
    print(res.success, res.x[-1])
solve_two_cells()

[REASONING] (turn 40):
Again S=0, even though feasible S=3 (since numbers 1 and 2 sum to 3). So model is flawed: The edge constraint row is -1*y0_1 -2*y0_2 -1*y1_1 -2*y1_2 + S <= 0. For assignment where cell0 gets 1 (y0_1=1) and cell1 gets 2 (y1_2=1), the left side becomes -1 -2 + S = S -3 <= 0 => S <= 3. So S can be up to 3. The solver should maximize S, but maybe due to integrality of S, but we set integrality for all variables, including S. So S integer. So optimum should be 3.

But solver returns 0. Something about objective: we set c[-1] = -1, but maybe the solver treats objective as minimize c^T x, but

def min_edge_sum(grid):
    n = grid.shape[0]
    min_sum = 10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1 < n:
                s = grid[i,j] + grid[i+1,j]
                if s < min_sum:
                    min_sum = s
            if j+1 < n:
                s = grid[i,j] + grid[i,j+1]
                if s < min_sum:
                    min_sum = s
    return min_sum

print(min_edge_sum(grid))

import random, math, time, numpy as np

n=60
N=n*n
# Precompute neighbor indices for each vertex (list of neighbor linear indices)
neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
def idx(i,j):
    return i*n + j

for i in range(n):
    for j in range(n):
        v = idx(i,j)
        if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
        if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
        if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
        if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))

def min_edge_sum_assign(assign):
    # assign is list of length N, numbers assigned to each vertex index
    min_sum = 10**9
    for v in range(N):
        a = assign[v]
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w>v: # count each edge once
                s = a + assign[w]
                if s < min_sum:
                    min_sum = s
    return min_sum

# initial assignment: degree-based as earlier
degrees = [len(neighbors[i]) for i in range(N)]
# sort vertices by degree descending, assign largest numbers to highest degree
verts_by_deg = sorted(range(N), key=lambda v: degrees[v], reverse=True)
assign = [0]*N
# assign numbers 1..N to verts_by_deg in increasing order? Actually we want largest numbers to highest degree.
for rank, v in enumerate(verts_by_deg):
    assign[v] = N - rank  # largest number to first (highest degree)
# compute min sum
current_min = min_edge_sum_assign(assign)
current_min

import random, math, time, numpy as np

n=60
N=n*n
# Precompute neighbor indices for each vertex (list of neighbor linear indices)
neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
def idx(i,j):
    return i*n + j

for i in range(n):
    for j in range(n):
        v = idx(i,j)
        if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
        if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
        if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
        if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))

def min_edge_sum_assign(assign):
    # assign is list of length N, numbers assigned to each vertex index
    min_sum = 10**9
    for v in range(N):
        a = assign[v]
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w>v: # count each edge once
                s = a + assign[w]
                if s < min_sum:
                    min_sum = s
    return min_sum

# sort vertices by degree descending, assign largest numbers to highest degree
verts_by_deg = sorted(range(N), key=lambda v: degrees[v], reverse=True)

[CODE] (turn 9):
import random, math, time, numpy as np

def simulated_annealing(time_limit=20.0):
    start = time.time()
    # start with degree-based assignment
    assign = [0]*N
    for rank, v in enumerate(verts_by_deg):
        assign[v] = N - rank
    # compute min sum
    min_sum = min_edge_sum_assign(assign)
    best_assign = assign[:]
    best_min = min_sum
    # temperature schedule
    T0 = 1000.0
    while time.time() - start < time_limit:
        # choose two random vertices to swap
        i, j = random.sample(range(N), 2)
        # compute effect on min sum quickly? We'll compute full after swap (cost small)
        assign[i], assign[j] = assign[j], assign[i]
        new_min = min_edge_sum_assign(assign)
        # acceptance criteria
        if new_min > min_sum or random.random() < math.exp((new_min - min_sum)/ (T0 * (1 - (time.time()-start)/time_limit))):
            min_sum = new_min
            if new_min > best_min:
                best_min = new_min
                best_assign = assign[:]
        else:
            # revert swap
            assign[i], assign[j] = assign[j], assign[i]
    return best_min, best_assign

best_min, best_assign = simulated_annealing(15.0)
best_min

import random, math, time, numpy as np

def simulated_annealing(time_limit=20.0):
    start = time.time()
    # start with degree-based assignment
    assign = [0]*N
    for rank, v in enumerate(verts_by_deg):
        assign[v] = N - rank
    # compute min sum
    min_sum = min_edge_sum_assign(assign)
    best_assign = assign[:]
    best_min = min_sum
    # temperature schedule
    T0 = 1000.0
    while time.time() - start < time_limit:
        # choose two random vertices to swap
        i, j = random.sample(range(N), 2)
        # compute effect on min sum quickly? We'll compute full after swap (cost small)
        assign[i], assign[j] = assign[j], assign[i]
        new_min = min_edge_sum_assign(assign)
        # acceptance criteria
        if new_min > min_sum or random.random() < math.exp((new_min - min_sum)/ (T0 * (1 - (time.time()-start)/time_limit))):
            min_sum = new_min
            if new_min > best_min:
                best_min = new_min
                best_assign = assign[:]
        else:
            # revert swap
            assign[i], assign[j] = assign[j], assign[i]
    return best_min, best_assign

A = np.zeros((E, Nvar))

for idx_edge, (u,v) in enumerate(edges):
    A[idx_edge, u] = -1
    A[idx_edge, v] = -1

[CODE] (turn 13):
import heapq, random, math, time, collections, itertools, sys, numpy as np

def feasible_S(S):
    n=60
    N=n*n
    # adjacency list
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    def idx(i,j): return i*n + j
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=idx(i,j)
            if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
            if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
            if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
            if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))
    # lower bounds
    LB = [1]*N
    # all vertices initially unassigned
    unassigned = set(range(N))
    # priority queue of (-LB, vertex) to get max LB
    heap = [(-LB[v], v) for v in range(N)]
    heapq.heapify(heap)
    # assign numbers descending
    for x in range(N, 0, -1):
        # pop until we get an unassigned vertex (since we may have duplicates)
        while heap:
            neglb, v = heapq.heappop(heap)
            if v in unassigned:
                break
        else:
            return False  # no vertex left
        # check LB
        if -neglb > x:
            return False
        # assign x to v
        unassigned.remove(v)
        # update neighbors' LB
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w in unassigned:
                new_lb = max(LB[w], S - x)
                if new_lb > LB[w]:
                    LB[w] = new_lb
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (-LB[w], w))
    # all assigned successfully
    return True

# test for some S values
for S in [3600, 3590, 3500, 3400, 3300, 3200]:
    print(S, feasible_S(S))

[CODE] (turn 13):
import heapq, random, math, time, collections, itertools, sys, numpy as np

def feasible_S(S):
    n=60
    N=n*n
    # adjacency list
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    def idx(i,j): return i*n + j
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=idx(i,j)
            if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
            if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
            if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
            if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))
    # lower bounds
    LB = [1]*N
    # all vertices initially unassigned
    unassigned = set(range(N))
    # priority queue of (-LB, vertex) to get max LB
    heap = [(-LB[v], v) for v in range(N)]
    heapq.heapify(heap)
    # assign numbers descending
    for x in range(N, 0, -1):
        # pop until we get an unassigned vertex (since we may have duplicates)
        while heap:
            neglb, v = heapq.heappop(heap)
            if v in unassigned:
                break
        else:
            return False  # no vertex left
        # check LB
        if -neglb > x:
            return False
        # assign x to v
        unassigned.remove(v)
        # update neighbors' LB
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w in unassigned:
                new_lb = max(LB[w], S - x)
                if new_lb > LB[w]:
                    LB[w] = new_lb
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (-LB[w], w))
    # all assigned successfully
    return True

# test for some S values
for S in [3600, 3590, 3500, 3400, 3300, 3200]:
    print(S, feasible_S(S))

def debug_feasible(S, steps=5):
    n=60
    N=n*n
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    def idx(i,j): return i*n + j
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=idx(i,j)
            if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
            if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
            if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
            if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))
    LB=[1]*N
    unassigned=set(range(N))
    heap=[(-LB[v], v) for v in range(N)]
    heapq.heapify(heap)
    for x in range(N,0,-1):
        while heap:
            neglb, v = heapq.heappop(heap)
            if v in unassigned:
                break
        else:
            print("no vertex left")
            return False
        if -neglb > x:
            print(f"fail at x={x}, vertex {v}, LB={-neglb}")
            return False
        # assign
        unassigned.remove(v)
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w in unassigned:
                new_lb = max(LB[w], S - x)
                if new_lb > LB[w]:
                    LB[w] = new_lb
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (-LB[w], w))
        if x % 500 == 0:
            print(f"assigned {x}, remaining {len(unassigned)}")
    print("success")
    return True

debug_feasible(1800)

def feasible_S_increasing(S):
    n=60
    N=n*n
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    def idx(i,j): return i*n + j
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=idx(i,j)
            if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
            if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
            if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
            if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))
    LB = [1]*N
    unassigned = set(range(N))
    # priority queue for smallest LB (min-heap)
    heap = [(LB[v], v) for v in range(N)]
    heapq.heapify(heap)
    for x in range(1, N+1):
        # get vertex with smallest LB that is unassigned
        while heap:
            lb, v = heapq.heappop(heap)
            if v in unassigned:
                break
        else:
            return False
        if lb > x:
            return False
        # assign x to v
        unassigned.remove(v)
        # update neighbors' LB
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w in unassigned:
                new_lb = max(LB[w], S - x)
                if new_lb > LB[w]:
                    LB[w] = new_lb
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (LB[w], w))
    return True

for S in [1800, 2000, 2500, 3000]:
    print(S, feasible_S_increasing(S))

def construct_assignment(S):
    n=60
    N=n*n
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    def idx(i,j): return i*n + j
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=idx(i,j)
            if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
            if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
            if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
            if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))
    LB = [1]*N
    unassigned = set(range(N))
    heap = [(LB[v], v) for v in range(N)]
    heapq.heapify(heap)
    assign = [0]*N
    for x in range(1, N+1):
        while heap:
            lb, v = heapq.heappop(heap)
            if v in unassigned:
                break
        else:
            raise Exception("no vertex")
        if lb > x:
            raise Exception(f"fail at x={x}, lb={lb}")
        assign[v] = x
        unassigned.remove(v)
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w in unassigned:
                new_lb = max(LB[w], S - x)
                if new_lb > LB[w]:
                    LB[w] = new_lb
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (LB[w], w))
    return assign

def min_edge_sum_from_assign(assign):
    n=60
    def idx(i,j): return i*n + j
    min_sum = 10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v = idx(i,j)
            if i+1<n:
                s = assign[v] + assign[idx(i+1,j)]
                if s < min_sum: min_sum = s
            if j+1<n:
                s = assign[v] + assign[idx(i,j+1)]
                if s < min_sum: min_sum = s
    return min_sum

assign = construct_assignment(4000)
print(min_edge_sum_from_assign(assign))

def feasible_S_correct(S):
    n=60
    N=n*n
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    def idx(i,j): return i*n + j
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=idx(i,j)
            if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
            if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
            if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
            if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))
    LB = [1]*N
    assign = [0]*N
    unassigned = set(range(N))
    heap = [(LB[v], v) for v in range(N)]
    heapq.heapify(heap)
    for x in range(1, N+1):
        # pop vertex with smallest LB
        while heap:
            lb, v = heapq.heappop(heap)
            if v in unassigned:
                break
        else:
            return False
        if lb > x:
            return False
        # assign
        assign[v] = x
        unassigned.remove(v)
        # update neighbors' LB based on this assignment
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w in unassigned:
                # neighbor's LB must be at least S - x (for future edge)
                new_lb = max(LB[w], S - x)
                if new_lb > LB[w]:
                    LB[w] = new_lb
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (LB[w], w))
            else:
                # neighbor already assigned, check constraint
                if assign[w] + x < S:
                    return False
    return True

for S in [1800, 2000, 2500, 3000, 3400, 3600]:
    print(S, feasible_S_correct(S))

def feasible_S_correct(S):

def construct_assignment_correct(S):
    n=60
    N=n*n
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    def idx(i,j): return i*n + j
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=idx(i,j)
            if i>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i-1,j))
            if i<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i+1,j))
            if j>0: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j-1))
            if j<n-1: neighbors[v].append(idx(i,j+1))
    LB = [1]*N
    assign = [0]*N
    unassigned = set(range(N))
    heap = [(LB[v], v) for v in range(N)]
    heapq.heapify(heap)
    for x in range(1, N+1):
        while heap:
            lb, v = heapq.heappop(heap)
            if v in unassigned:
                break
        else:
            raise Exception("no vertex")
        if lb > x:
            raise Exception(f"fail at x={x}, lb={lb}")
        assign[v] = x
        unassigned.remove(v)
        for w in neighbors[v]:
            if w in unassigned:
                new_lb = max(LB[w], S - x)
                if new_lb > LB[w]:
                    LB[w] = new_lb
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (LB[w], w))
            else:
                if assign[w] + x < S:
                    raise Exception(f"edge violation between {v} and {w}, sum {assign[w]+x} < {S}")
    return assign

[CODE] (turn 23):
import random, math, time, numpy as np

n=60
N=n*n
def idx(i,j): return i*n + j

# initial assignment: simple bipartition (black low, white high)
grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
# chessboard colors: (i+j)%2 == 0 -> black
black_cells = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0]
white_cells = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==1]
# assign low numbers to black, high to white
low_numbers = list(range(1, len(black_cells)+1))
high_numbers = list(range(len(black_cells)+1, N+1))
# assign arbitrarily
for (i,j), num in zip(black_cells, low_numbers):
    grid[i,j] = num
for (i,j), num in zip(white_cells, high_numbers):
    grid[i,j] = num

def min_edge_sum_grid(g):
    n = g.shape[0]
    min_sum = 10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1 < n:
                s = g[i,j] + g[i+1,j]
                if s < min_sum: min_sum = s
            if j+1 < n:
                s = g[i,j] + g[i,j+1]
                if s < min_sum: min_sum = s
    return min_sum

print("initial min sum:", min_edge_sum_grid(grid))

import random, math, time, numpy as np

n=60
N=n*n
def idx(i,j): return i*n + j

# initial assignment: simple bipartition (black low, white high)
grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
# chessboard colors: (i+j)%2 == 0 -> black
black_cells = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0]
white_cells = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==1]
# assign low numbers to black, high to white
low_numbers = list(range(1, len(black_cells)+1))
high_numbers = list(range(len(black_cells)+1, N+1))
# assign arbitrarily
for (i,j), num in zip(black_cells, low_numbers):
    grid[i,j] = num
for (i,j), num in zip(white_cells, high_numbers):
    grid[i,j] = num

def min_edge_sum_grid(g):
    n = g.shape[0]
    min_sum = 10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1 < n:
                s = g[i,j] + g[i+1,j]
                if s < min_sum: min_sum = s
            if j+1 < n:
                s = g[i,j] + g[i,j+1]
                if s < min_sum: min_sum = s
    return min_sum

print("initial min sum:", min_edge_sum_grid(grid))

[CODE] (turn 24):
def simulated_annealing_grid(grid, time_limit=30.0):
    n = grid.shape[0]
    best_grid = grid.copy()
    best_min = min_edge_sum_grid(grid)
    cur_grid = grid.copy()
    cur_min = best_min
    start = time.time()
    # precompute neighbor list for each cell index
    neighbors = {}
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx = i*n + j
            neigh = []
            if i>0: neigh.append(((i-1)*n + j))
            if i+1<n: neigh.append(((i+1)*n + j))
            if j>0: neigh.append((i*n + (j-1)))
            if j+1<n: neigh.append((i*n + (j+1)))
            neighbors[idx] = neigh
    # flatten grid to 1D array for faster swaps
    arr = cur_grid.ravel()
    N = arr.size
    # function to compute min edge sum efficiently? We'll recompute full each time for simplicity
    while time.time() - start < time_limit:
        # pick two random positions to swap
        i1, i2 = random.sample(range(N), 2)
        # swap
        arr[i1], arr[i2] = arr[i2], arr[i1]
        # compute new min sum
        new_min = min_edge_sum_grid(arr.reshape(n,n))
        # accept if better or with probability
        if new_min >= cur_min or random.random() < 0.001:
            cur_min = new_min
            if new_min > best_min:
                best_min = new_min
                best_grid = arr.copy()
        else:
            # revert swap
            arr[i1], arr[i2] = arr[i2], arr[i1]
    return best_min, best_grid.reshape(n,n)

best_min, best_grid = simulated_annealing_grid(grid, time_limit=20.0)
print("best min sum after SA:", best_min)

[CODE] (turn 24):
def simulated_annealing_grid(grid, time_limit=30.0):
    n = grid.shape[0]
    best_grid = grid.copy()
    best_min = min_edge_sum_grid(grid)
    cur_grid = grid.copy()
    cur_min = best_min
    start = time.time()
    # precompute neighbor list for each cell index
    neighbors = {}
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx = i*n + j
            neigh = []
            if i>0: neigh.append(((i-1)*n + j))
            if i+1<n: neigh.append(((i+1)*n + j))
            if j>0: neigh.append((i*n + (j-1)))
            if j+1<n: neigh.append((i*n + (j+1)))
            neighbors[idx] = neigh
    # flatten grid to 1D array for faster swaps
    arr = cur_grid.ravel()
    N = arr.size
    # function to compute min edge sum efficiently? We'll recompute full each time for simplicity
    while time.time() - start < time_limit:
        # pick two random positions to swap
        i1, i2 = random.sample(range(N), 2)
        # swap
        arr[i1], arr[i2] = arr[i2], arr[i1]
        # compute new min sum
        new_min = min_edge_sum_grid(arr.reshape(n,n))
        # accept if better or with probability
        if new_min >= cur_min or random.random() < 0.001:
            cur_min = new_min
            if new_min > best_min:
                best_min = new_min
                best_grid = arr.copy()
        else:
            # revert swap
            arr[i1], arr[i2] = arr[i2], arr[i1]
    return best_min, best_grid.reshape(n,n)

best_min, best_grid = simulated_annealing_grid(grid, time_limit=20.0)
print("best min sum after SA:", best_min)

[REASONING] (turn 26):
We can attempt to derive an upper bound using degree-weighted sum.

Consider each edge sum >= S. Sum over all edges >= S*E = S*7080.

But sum over all edges = sum_{v} deg(v) * a_v = D.

Thus D >= S*E => S <= D / E.

Now D depends on assignment. The maximum possible D is D_max = 25899224 computed earlier, giving S <= 25899224 / 7080 ≈ 3658.08. So S <= 3658 (integer). That's an upper bound.

Now we need a lower bound: we have construction achieving S=1802. So S is between 1802 and 3658.

We need to find maximum possible S. Could it be as high as 3658? Let's attempt to construct assignment achieving S close to 3658 using heuristic optimization (maybe simulated annealing with objective to maximize min edge sum). Our earlier simple random swaps didn't improve much, but we can try more advanced approach: start with assignment that puts largest numbers on interior (degree 4) and small numbers on corners (degree 2). That gave min sum 3. Not good. We need to ensure low numbers not adjacent.

Idea: Use bipartition assignment but also reorder numbers within each color to increase min sum. Since edges always connect opposite colors, the sum of numbers on an edge is (black number) + (white number). The minimal sum is min_{black b, white w adjacent} (b + w). Since each black vertex has up to 4 white neighbors, the minimal sum will be determined by some pair where black number is low and its neighbor white number is also relatively low.

Thus to maximize minimal sum, we should assign the low black numbers to black vertices whose white neighbors are all high numbers. Similarly, low white numbers should be placed at white vertices whose black neighbors are high.

Thus we can treat it as a matching problem: we need to pair each low black number with a set of white numbers (its neighbors). Since each black vertex has multiple neighbors, we need to ensure each black low number's neighbors are among the highest white numbers.

Similarly for low white numbers.

Since each white vertex may be neighbor to up to 4 black vertices, we need to allocate high numbers accordingly.

Potentially we can assign the 1800 smallest numbers to black squares, but arrange them such that each black low number's neighboring white squares have the largest white numbers. Since each white vertex can be neighbor to up to 4 black vertices, we could assign the largest white numbers to the white squares that are adjacent to many low black squares.

Thus we could try to solve a bipartite assignment problem: assign white numbers to white vertices to maximize the minimal sum across edges given black numbers fixed (low numbers). Equivalent to: we have black numbers a_b (given), we need to assign white numbers b_w

from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
import numpy as np, math, itertools, sys, time, random
