[CODE] (turn 3):
def max_S_grid(m, n):
    N = m*n
    # adjacency edges (undirected)
    edges = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            v = i*n + j
            if i+1 < m:
                edges.append((v, (i+1)*n + j))
            if j+1 < n:
                edges.append((v, i*n + (j+1)))
    # binary search S
    low, high = 2, 2*N  # inclusive bounds
    best = 0
    while low <= high:
        mid = (low+high)//2
        feasible = check_feasible(m,n,edges,mid)
        if feasible:
            best = mid
            low = mid+1
        else:
            high = mid-1
    return best

def check_feasible(m,n,edges,S):
    N = m*n
    # variables y_{v,k} flattened: index = v*N + (k-1)
    num_vars = N*N
    # constraints: each vertex assigned exactly one number
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    # each vertex
    for v in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[v*N + (k-1)] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one vertex
    for k in range(1,N+1):
        row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        for v in range(N):
            row[v*N + (k-1)] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # inequality constraints for edges low-sum pairs
    A_ub = []
    b_ub = []
    for (u,v) in edges:
        for i in range(1,N+1):
            for j in range(1,N+1):
                if i + j < S:
                    row = np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
                    row[u*N + (i-1)] = 1
                    row[v*N + (j-1)] = 1
                    A_ub.append(row)
                    b_ub.append(1)
    # Convert to numpy arrays
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq, dtype=int)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq, dtype=int)
    if len(A_ub)>0:
        A_ub = np.array(A_ub, dtype=int)
        b_ub = np.array(b_ub, dtype=int)
    else:
        A_ub = None
        b_ub = None
    # bounds: binary variables 0 <= y <= 1
    bounds = Bounds(0,1)
    # integer variables: all variables integer (binary)
    integrality = np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # objective: dummy zero vector (feasibility)
    c = np.zeros(num_vars)
    try:
        res = milp(c, integrality=integrality,
                   bounds=bounds,
                   constraints=[
                       LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq),
                       LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub) if A_ub is not None else None
                   ])
    except Exception as e:
        print("MILP error", e)
        return False
    if res.success:
        return True
    else:
        return False

print(max_S_grid(2,2))

def max_S_grid(m, n):
    N = m*n
    # adjacency edges (undirected)
    edges = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            v = i*n + j
            if i+1 < m:
                edges.append((v, (i+1)*n + j))
            if j+1 < n:
                edges.append((v, i*n + (j+1)))
    # binary search S
    low, high = 2, 2*N  # inclusive bounds
    best = 0
    while low <= high:
        mid = (low+high)//2
        feasible = check_feasible(m,n,edges,mid)
        if feasible:
            best = mid
            low = mid+1
        else:
            high = mid-1
    return best

def get_solution_grid(m,n,S):
    N=m*n
    edges=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            v=i*n+j
            if i+1<m:
                edges.append((v,(i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((v,i*n+(j+1)))
    # Build MILP as before but also retrieve solution
    num_vars=N*N
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    for v in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    for k in range(1,N+1):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
        for v in range(N):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq); b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    A_ub=[]; b_ub=[]
    for (u,v) in edges:
        for i in range(1,N+1):
            for j in range(1,N+1):
                if i+j < S:
                    row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
                    row[u*N+(i-1)]=1
                    row[v*N+(j-1)]=1
                    A_ub.append(row); b_ub.append(1)
    if A_ub:
        A_ub=np.array(A_ub); b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    else:
        A_ub=None; b_ub=None
    bounds=Bounds(0,1)
    integrality=np.ones(num_vars,dtype=int)
    c=np.zeros(num_vars)
    cons=[]
    cons.append(LinearConstraint(A_eq,b_eq,b_eq))
    if A_ub is not None:
        cons.append(LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf,b_ub))
    res=milp(c,integrality=integrality,bounds=bounds,constraints=cons)
    if not res.success:
        return None
    y=res.x
    # Build grid
    grid=np.zeros((m,n),dtype=int)
    for v in range(N):
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            if round(y[v*N+(k-1)])>0.5:
                i=v//n; j=v%n
                grid[i,j]=k
    return grid

grid=get_solution_grid(3,3,9)
grid

[CODE] (turn 6):
def min_adjacent_sum(grid):
    m,n=grid.shape
    mins=10**9
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1<m:
                mins=min(mins,grid[i,j]+grid[i+1,j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins=min(mins,grid[i,j]+grid[i,j+1])
    return mins

min_adjacent_sum(grid)

import numpy as np, math, itertools, sys, time
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def check_feasible(m,n,edges,S):
    N=m*n
    num_vars=N*N
    # equality constraints
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    for v in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    for k in range(1,N+1):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
        for v in range(N):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq); b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # inequality constraints
    A_ub=[]; b_ub=[]
    for (u,v) in edges:
        for i in range(1,N+1):
            for j in range(1,N+1):
                if i+j < S:
                    row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
                    row[u*N+(i-1)]=1
                    row[v*N+(j-1)]=1
                    A_ub.append(row); b_ub.append(1)
    if A_ub:
        A_ub=np.array(A_ub); b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    else:
        A_ub=None; b_ub=None
    bounds=Bounds(0,1)
    integrality=np.ones(num_vars,dtype=int)
    c=np.zeros(num_vars)
    cons=[LinearConstraint(A_eq,b_eq,b_eq)]
    if A_ub is not None:
        cons.append(LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf,b_ub))
    res=milp(c,integrality=integrality,bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'presolve':True})
    return res.success

def max_S_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    edges=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            v=i*n+j
            if i+1<m:
                edges.append((v,(i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((v,i*n+(j+1)))
    low,high=2,2*N
    best=0
    while low<=high:
        mid=(low+high)//2
        if check_feasible(m,n,edges,mid):
            best=mid
            low=mid+1
        else:
            high=mid-1
    return best

print(max_S_grid(2,6))

def check_feasible(m,n,edges,S):

    num_vars=N*N
    # equality constraints
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    for v in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)

            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    for k in range(1,N+1):

        for v in range(N):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq); b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # inequality constraints

            for j in range(1,N+1):

                    row[v*N+(j-1)]=1
                    A_ub.append(row); b_ub.append(1)
    if A_ub:

    res=milp(c,integrality=integrality,bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'presolve':True})
    return res.success

def max_S_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    edges=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            v=i*n+j
            if i+1<m:
                edges.append((v,(i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((v,i*n+(j+1)))

        mid=(low+high)//2
        if check_feasible(m,n,edges,mid):

            low=mid+1

[CODE] (turn 22):
def check_feasible(m,n,edges,S):
    N=m*n
    num_vars=N*N
    # Build equality constraints
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    for v in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    for k in range(1,N+1):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
        for v in range(N):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq); b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # inequality constraints
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (u,v) in edges:
        for i in range(1,N+1):
            for j in range(1,N+1):
                if i+j < S:
                    row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
                    row[u*N+(i-1)]=1
                    row[v*N+(j-1)]=1
                    A_ub.append(row); b_ub.append(1)
    if A_ub:
        A_ub=np.array(A_ub); b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    else:
        A_ub=None; b_ub=None
    bounds=Bounds(0,1)
    integrality=np.ones(num_vars,dtype=int)
    c=np.zeros(num_vars)
    cons=[LinearConstraint(A_eq,b_eq,b_eq)]
    if A_ub is not None:
        cons.append(LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf,b_ub))
    res=milp(c,integrality=integrality,bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'presolve':True})
    return res.success

def check_feasible(m,n,edges,S):
    N=m*n
    num_vars=N*N
    # Build equality constraints
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    for v in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
        for k in range(1,N+1):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    for k in range(1,N+1):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
        for v in range(N):
            row[v*N+(k-1)]=1
        A_eq.append(row); b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq); b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # inequality constraints
    A_ub=[]
    b_ub=[]
    for (u,v) in edges:
        for i in range(1,N+1):
            for j in range(1,N+1):
                if i+j < S:
                    row=np.zeros(num_vars,dtype=int)
                    row[u*N+(i-1)]=1
                    row[v*N+(j-1)]=1
                    A_ub.append(row); b_ub.append(1)
    if A_ub:
        A_ub=np.array(A_ub); b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
    else:
        A_ub=None; b_ub=None
    bounds=Bounds(0,1)
    integrality=np.ones(num_vars,dtype=int)
    c=np.zeros(num_vars)

    if A_ub is not None:
        cons.append(LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf,b_ub))
    res=milp(c,integrality=integrality,bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'presolve':True})

def max_S_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    edges=[]
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            v=i*n+j
            if i+1<m:
                edges.append((v,(i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((v,i*n+(j+1)))
    low,high=2,2*N

        mid=(low+high)//2
        if check_feasible(m,n,edges,mid):
            best=mid
            low=mid+1
        else:
            high=mid-1

[CODE] (turn 32):
def min_deg_sum(N=3600):
    interior=3364
    border=232
    corners=4
    # assign numbers
    # interior numbers 1..interior
    sum_interior = 4 * sum(range(1, interior+1))
    # border numbers interior+1 .. interior+border
    start = interior+1
    end = interior+border
    sum_border = 3 * sum(range(start, end+1))
    # corners numbers end+1 .. N
    start2 = end+1
    sum_corners = 2 * sum(range(start2, N+1))
    total = sum_interior + sum_border + sum_corners
    return total

min_sum = min_deg_sum()
min_sum

    # corners numbers end+1 .. N
    start2 = end+1
    sum_corners = 2 * sum(range(start2, N+1))
    total = sum_interior + sum_border + sum_corners

[CODE] (turn 34):
def construct_grid(m,n):
    interior = (m-2)*(n-2)
    border = 2*m + 2*n - 4 - 4  # border non-corner count? Actually total border cells = 2*m + 2*n - 4
    # compute counts
    total = m*n
    # assign numbers
    # interior cells: degree 4
    # border non-corner: degree 3
    # corners: degree 2
    # We'll fill row-major: corners first? We'll assign based on position.
    grid = np.zeros((m,n), dtype=int)
    # Determine lists of positions by degree
    positions = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            deg = 4
            if i==0 or i==m-1:
                deg -=1
            if j==0 or j==n-1:
                deg -=1
            positions.append(((i,j), deg))
    # Sort positions by degree descending (higher degree first)
    positions.sort(key=lambda x: -x[1])
    # Assign numbers 1..total in that order
    for idx, ((i,j), deg) in enumerate(positions):
        grid[i,j] = idx+1
    return grid

grid = construct_grid(6,6)
grid

[CODE] (turn 34):
def construct_grid(m,n):
    interior = (m-2)*(n-2)
    border = 2*m + 2*n - 4 - 4  # border non-corner count? Actually total border cells = 2*m + 2*n - 4
    # compute counts
    total = m*n
    # assign numbers
    # interior cells: degree 4
    # border non-corner: degree 3
    # corners: degree 2
    # We'll fill row-major: corners first? We'll assign based on position.
    grid = np.zeros((m,n), dtype=int)
    # Determine lists of positions by degree
    positions = []
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            deg = 4
            if i==0 or i==m-1:
                deg -=1
            if j==0 or j==n-1:
                deg -=1
            positions.append(((i,j), deg))
    # Sort positions by degree descending (higher degree first)
    positions.sort(key=lambda x: -x[1])
    # Assign numbers 1..total in that order
    for idx, ((i,j), deg) in enumerate(positions):
        grid[i,j] = idx+1
    return grid

grid = construct_grid(6,6)

import math
N=3600
# counts
cnt_int=3364
cnt_edge=232
cnt_corner=4
# assign numbers descending
nums=list(range(1,N+1))
# assign largest to interior
assigned_int=nums[-cnt_int:]  # largest cnt_int numbers
assigned_edge=nums[-(cnt_int+cnt_edge):-cnt_int]  # next largest
assigned_corner=nums[:cnt_corner]  # smallest cnt_corner numbers
# compute sum deg*value
sum_val = 4*sum(assigned_int) + 3*sum(assigned_edge) + 2*sum(assigned_corner)
sum_val

[CODE] (turn 1):
import math
N=3600
# counts
cnt_int=3364
cnt_edge=232
cnt_corner=4
# assign numbers descending
nums=list(range(1,N+1))
# assign largest to interior
assigned_int=nums[-cnt_int:]  # largest cnt_int numbers
assigned_edge=nums[-(cnt_int+cnt_edge):-cnt_int]  # next largest
assigned_corner=nums[:cnt_corner]  # smallest cnt_corner numbers
# compute sum deg*value
sum_val = 4*sum(assigned_int) + 3*sum(assigned_edge) + 2*sum(assigned_corner)
sum_val

import random, math, time, sys, itertools, collections, numpy as np, math, sys, time, itertools, random, math
def min_edge_sum(grid):
    n=60
    # compute min sum across horizontal and vertical edges
    min_sum=10**9
    # horizontal edges
    for i in range(n):
        row=grid[i]
        for j in range(n-1):
            s=row[j]+row[j+1]
            if s<min_sum:
                min_sum=s
    # vertical edges
    for i in range(n-1):
        for j in range(n):
            s=grid[i][j]+grid[i+1][j]
            if s<min_sum:
                min_sum=s
    return min_sum

def initial_grid():
    # start with numbers 1..3600 placed randomly
    nums=list(range(1,3600+1))
    random.shuffle(nums)
    grid=[nums[i*60:(i+1)*60] for i in range(60)]
    return grid

def simulated_annealing(iterations=200000, start_temp=5.0, end_temp=0.01):
    grid=initial_grid()
    best_grid=[row[:] for row in grid]
    best=min_edge_sum(grid)
    cur=min_edge_sum(grid)
    n=60
    temp=start_temp
    for it in range(iterations):
        # linear cooling
        temp = start_temp * ((end_temp/start_temp) ** (it/iterations))
        # pick two random cells
        i1,j1 = random.randrange(n), random.randrange(n)
        i2,j2 = random.randrange(n), random.randrange(n)
        if i1==i2 and j1==j2:
            continue
        # swap
        grid[i1][j1], grid[i2][j2] = grid[i2][j2], grid[i1][j1]
        new_min = min_edge_sum(grid)
        delta = new_min - cur
        # accept if better or with probability exp(delta/temp) if delta negative? Actually we want maximize min, so delta positive is improvement.
        if delta>0 or random.random() < math.exp(delta / temp):
            cur = new_min
            if cur > best:
                best = cur
                best_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
        else:
            # revert swap
            grid[i1][j1], grid[i2][j2] = grid[i2][j2], grid[i1][j1]
        # optional early stop if best reaches theoretical bound
        if best >= 3597:
            break
    return best, best_grid

best_val, best_grid = simulated_annealing(iterations=200000)
best_val

import numpy as np, random, math, time, sys, itertools, collections, math, random, time, numpy as np
def min_edge_sum_np(arr):
    # arr shape (60,60)
    # compute sums of horizontal edges
    horiz = arr[:, :-1] + arr[:, 1:]
    vert = arr[:-1, :] + arr[1:, :]
    return min(horiz.min(), vert.min())

def simulated_annealing_fast(iterations=200000, start_temp=5.0, end_temp=0.01):
    n=60
    nums = np.arange(1, n*n+1)
    np.random.shuffle(nums)
    arr = nums.reshape((n,n))
    best_arr = arr.copy()
    best = min_edge_sum_np(arr)
    cur = best
    for it in range(iterations):
        temp = start_temp * ((end_temp/start_temp) ** (it/iterations))
        # pick two random positions
        i1,j1 = random.randrange(n), random.randrange(n)
        i2,j2 = random.randrange(n), random.randrange(n)
        if i1==i2 and j1==j2:
            continue
        # swap
        arr[i1,j1], arr[i2,j2] = arr[i2,j2], arr[i1,j1]
        new = min_edge_sum_np(arr)
        delta = new - cur
        if delta > 0 or random.random() < math.exp(delta / temp):
            cur = new
            if cur > best:
                best = cur
                best_arr = arr.copy()
        else:
            # revert
            arr[i1,j1], arr[i2,j2] = arr[i2,j2], arr[i1,j1]
        if best >= 3597:
            break
    return best, best_arr

best_val, best_arr = simulated_annealing_fast(iterations=50000)
best_val

import numpy as np, random, math, time, sys, itertools, collections, math, random, time, numpy as np
def min_edge_sum_np(arr):
    # arr shape (60,60)
    # compute sums of horizontal edges
    horiz = arr[:, :-1] + arr[:, 1:]
    vert = arr[:-1, :] + arr[1:, :]
    return min(horiz.min(), vert.min())

def simulated_annealing_fast(iterations=200000, start_temp=5.0, end_temp=0.01):

import itertools, math, time, sys, numpy as np, random, collections, itertools, math, sys, time, numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_S_grid(n):
    N=n*n
    # cells indexed 0..N-1
    # adjacency list
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx = i*n + j
            if i>0: adj[idx].append((i-1)*n + j)
            if i<n-1: adj[idx].append((i+1)*n + j)
            if j>0: adj[idx].append(i*n + (j-1))
            if j<n-1: adj[idx].append(i*n + (j+1))
    # Precompute edges list (unique)
    edges = []
    for u in range(N):
        for v in adj[u]:
            if u < v:
                edges.append((u,v))
    E=len(edges)
    # binary variables x_{i,k} for each cell i and number k (1..N)
    # We'll use MILP with integer variables (0/1)
    # constraints:
    # each cell assigned exactly one number: sum_k x_{i,k}=1
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell: sum_i x_{i,k}=1
    # For each edge (u,v): sum_{k} k*x_{u,k} + sum_{k} k*x_{v,k} >= S
    # This is linear because sum k*x_{i,k} is the value assigned to cell i.
    # We'll binary search S.
    # Build coefficient matrix for constraints.
    # Variables ordering: first N*N variables.
    total_vars = N*N
    # Build constraints matrices once (except RHS depends on S)
    # We'll use sparse representation via scipy.sparse? Not needed for small n.
    import numpy as np
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    # cell constraints
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # number constraints
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for i in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq)
    # inequality constraints for edges: sum k*x_{u,k} + sum k*x_{v,k} >= S
    # We'll represent as - (sum ...) <= -S
    # We'll create A_ub matrix with rows for each edge.
    A_ub = []
    b_ub = []
    # Precompute coefficient for each variable in edge constraint: for variable x_{i,k}, coefficient = k (value) for each incident edge.
    # We'll build each edge row.
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            val = (k+1)  # numbers start at 1
            row[u*N + k] = val
            row[v*N + k] = val
        A_ub.append(-row)  # because we will set <= -S
        b_ub.append(-0)  # placeholder, will set later
    A_ub = np.array(A_ub)
    # Now binary search S
    low=0
    high=2*N  # max possible sum
    best=0
    while low<=high:
        mid=(low+high)//2
        # set RHS for each edge: -mid
        b_ub_mid = np.full(len(edges), -mid, dtype=int)
        # Build MILP problem
        c = np.zeros(total_vars)  # objective zero
        # integrality: all variables integer (binary)
        integrality = np.ones(total_vars, dtype=int)
        bounds = Bounds(0,1)
        # Use milp
        res = milp(c=c,
                   A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq,
                   A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub_mid,
                   integrality=integrality,
                   bounds=bounds,
                   options={'disp': False})
        if res.success:
            best=mid
            low=mid+1
        else:
            high=mid-1
    return best

print(max_S_grid(4))

import itertools, math, time, sys, numpy as np, random, collections, itertools, math, sys, time, numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_S_grid(n):
    N=n*n
    # cells indexed 0..N-1
    # adjacency list
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx = i*n + j
            if i>0: adj[idx].append((i-1)*n + j)
            if i<n-1: adj[idx].append((i+1)*n + j)
            if j>0: adj[idx].append(i*n + (j-1))
            if j<n-1: adj[idx].append(i*n + (j+1))
    # Precompute edges list (unique)
    edges = []
    for u in range(N):
        for v in adj[u]:
            if u < v:
                edges.append((u,v))
    E=len(edges)
    # binary variables x_{i,k} for each cell i and number k (1..N)
    # We'll use MILP with integer variables (0/1)
    # constraints:
    # each cell assigned exactly one number: sum_k x_{i,k}=1
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell: sum_i x_{i,k}=1
    # For each edge (u,v): sum_{k} k*x_{u,k} + sum_{k} k*x_{v,k} >= S
    # This is linear because sum k*x_{i,k} is the value assigned to cell i.
    # We'll binary search S.
    # Build coefficient matrix for constraints.
    # Variables ordering: first N*N variables.
    total_vars = N*N
    # Build constraints matrices once (except RHS depends on S)
    # We'll use sparse representation via scipy.sparse? Not needed for small n.
    import numpy as np
    A_eq = []
    b_eq = []
    # cell constraints
    for i in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # number constraints
    for k in range(N):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for i in range(N):
            row[i*N + k] = 1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq = np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq)
    # inequality constraints for edges: sum k*x_{u,k} + sum k*x_{v,k} >= S
    # We'll represent as - (sum ...) <= -S
    # We'll create A_ub matrix with rows for each edge.
    A_ub = []
    b_ub = []
    # Precompute coefficient for each variable in edge constraint: for variable x_{i,k}, coefficient = k (value) for each incident edge.
    # We'll build each edge row.
    for (u,v) in edges:
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for k in range(N):
            val = (k+1)  # numbers start at 1
            row[u*N + k] = val
            row[v*N + k] = val
        A_ub.append(-row)  # because we will set <= -S
        b_ub.append(-0)  # placeholder, will set later
    A_ub = np.array(A_ub)
    # Now binary search S
    low=0
    high=2*N  # max possible sum
    best=0
    while low<=high:
        mid=(low+high)//2
        # set RHS for each edge: -mid
        b_ub_mid = np.full(len(edges), -mid, dtype=int)
        # Build MILP problem
        c = np.zeros(total_vars)  # objective zero
        # integrality: all variables integer (binary)
        integrality = np.ones(total_vars, dtype=int)
        bounds = Bounds(0,1)
        # Use milp
        res = milp(c=c,
                   A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq,
                   A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub_mid,
                   integrality=integrality,
                   bounds=bounds,
                   options={'disp': False})
        if res.success:
            best=mid
            low=mid+1
        else:
            high=mid-1
    return best

[OUTPUT] (turn 7):
Mixed-integer linear programming

    Solves problems of the following form:

    .. math::

        \min_x \ & c^T x \\
        \mbox{such that} \ & b_l \leq A x \leq b_u,\\
        & l \leq x \leq u, \\
        & x_i \in \mathbb{Z}, i \in X_i

    where :math:`x` is a vector of decision variables;
    :math:`c`, :math:`b_l`, :math:`b_u`, :math:`l`, and :math:`u` are vectors;
    :math:`A` is a matrix, and :math:`X_i` is the set of indices of
    decision variables that must be integral. (In this context, a
    variable that can assume only integer values is said to be "integral";
    it has an "integrality" constraint.)

    Alternatively, that's:

    minimize::

        c @ x

    such that::

        b_l <= A @ x <= b_u
        l <= x <= u
        Specified elements of x must be integers

    By default, ``l = 0`` and ``u = np.inf`` unless specified with
    ``bounds``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    c : 1D dense array_like
        The coefficients of the linear objective function to be minimized.
        `c` is converted to a double precision array before the problem is
        solved.
    integrality : 1D dense array_like, optional
        Indicates the type of integrality constraint on each decision variable.

        ``0`` : Continuous variable; no integrality constraint.

        ``1`` : Integer variable; decision variable must be an integer
        within `bounds`.

        ``2`` : Semi-continuous variable; decision variable must be within
        `bounds` or take value ``0``.
... (205 lines total)

[CODE] (turn 8):
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
def max_S_grid_small(n):
    N=n*n
    # adjacency edges
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if i+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, (i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1)))
    E=len(edges)
    total_vars=N*N
    # Build constraints matrices as sparse maybe using scipy.sparse
    import numpy as np, scipy.sparse as sp
    rows=[]
    cols=[]
    data=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # cell constraints
    for i in range(N):
        for k in range(N):
            rows.append(i)
            cols.append(i*N + k)
            data.append(1)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # number constraints
    offset = N
    for k in range(N):
        for i in range(N):
            rows.append(offset + k)
            cols.append(i*N + k)
            data.append(1)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq = sp.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*N, total_vars))
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq, dtype=int)
    # Edge constraints: sum k*x_{u,k}+sum k*x_{v,k} >= S
    # We'll create A_ub for lower bound using LinearConstraint with lower bound S
    # We'll create matrix A_edge where each row corresponds to edge, columns as variables, coefficient = (k+1) for both u and v.
    rows_e=[]
    cols_e=[]
    data_e=[]
    for e_idx,(u,v) in enumerate(edges):
        for k in range(N):
            coeff = k+1
            rows_e.append(e_idx)
            cols_e.append(u*N + k)
            data_e.append(coeff)
            rows_e.append(e_idx)
            cols_e.append(v*N + k)
            data_e.append(coeff)
    A_edge = sp.csr_matrix((data_e, (rows_e, cols_e)), shape=(E, total_vars))
    # We'll binary search S
    low=0
    high=2*N
    best=0
    while low<=high:
        mid=(low+high)//2
        # lower bound for each edge >= mid
        lc = np.full(E, mid, dtype=int)
        uc = np.full(E, np.iinfo(np.int64).max, dtype=int)  # no upper bound
        edge_constraint = LinearConstraint(A_edge, lc, uc)
        # combine with equality constraints using list
        constraints = [LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq), edge_constraint]
        # objective zero
        c = np.zeros(total_vars)
        integrality = np.ones(total_vars, dtype=int)
        bounds = Bounds(0,1)
        res = milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, integrality=integrality, bounds=bounds, options={'disp': False})
        if res.success:
            best=mid
            low=mid+1
        else:
            high=mid-1
    return best

print(max_S_grid_small(3))

    # adjacency edges

    for i in range(n):

            if i+1<n:

            if j+1<n:

    total_vars=N*N

    import numpy as np, scipy.sparse as sp
    rows=[]
    cols=[]
    data=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # cell constraints
    for i in range(N):
        for k in range(N):
            rows.append(i)
            cols.append(i*N + k)
            data.append(1)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # number constraints
    offset = N
    for k in range(N):
        for i in range(N):
            rows.append(offset + k)
            cols.append(i*N + k)
            data.append(1)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq = sp.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*N, total_vars))
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq, dtype=int)
    # Edge constraints: sum k*x_{u,k}+sum k*x_{v,k} >= S
    # We'll create A_ub for lower bound using LinearConstraint with lower bound S
    # We'll create matrix A_edge where each row corresponds to edge, columns as variables, coefficient = (k+1) for both u and v.
    rows_e=[]
    cols_e=[]
    data_e=[]
    for e_idx,(u,v) in enumerate(edges):
        for k in range(N):
            coeff = k+1
            rows_e.append(e_idx)
            cols_e.append(u*N + k)
            data_e.append(coeff)
            rows_e.append(e_idx)
            cols_e.append(v*N + k)
            data_e.append(coeff)
    A_edge = sp.csr_matrix((data_e, (rows_e, cols_e)), shape=(E, total_vars))
    # We'll binary search S
    low=0
    high=2*N
    best=0
    while low<=high:
        mid=(low+high)//2
        # lower bound for each edge >= mid
        lc = np.full(E, mid, dtype=int)
        uc = np.full(E, np.iinfo(np.int64).max, dtype=int)  # no upper bound
        edge_constraint = LinearConstraint(A_edge, lc, uc)
        # combine with equality constraints using list
        constraints = [LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq), edge_constraint]
        # objective zero
        c = np.zeros(total_vars)
        integrality = np.ones(total_vars, dtype=int)
        bounds = Bounds(0,1)
        res = milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, integrality=integrality, bounds=bounds, options={'disp': False})
        if res.success:
            best=mid
            low=mid+1
        else:
            high=mid-1
    return best

print(max_S_grid_small(3))

[CODE] (turn 10):
import itertools, math, sys, time, numpy as np

def max_S_bruteforce_3():
    n=3
    N=n*n
    best=0
    best_perm=None
    cells = list(range(N))
    # adjacency edges
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if i+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, (i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1))
                )
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        # compute min sum
        min_sum=10**9
        for u,v in edges:
            s=perm[u]+perm[v]
            if s<min_sum:
                min_sum=s
                if min_sum<=best: # prune
                    break
        else:
            # all edges checked
            if min_sum>best:
                best=min_sum
                best_perm=perm
    return best, best_perm

best, perm = max_S_bruteforce_3()
best

def min_sum_of_perm(perm, n):
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if i+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, (i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1)))
    return min(perm[u]+perm[v] for u,v in edges)

[CODE] (turn 12):
def max_S_grid_milp(n):
    N=n*n
    # edges
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if i+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, (i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1)))
    E=len(edges)
    total_vars=N*N
    import scipy.sparse as sp
    # Build equality constraints matrix
    rows=[]
    cols=[]
    data=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell assigned exactly one number
    for i in range(N):
        for k in range(N):
            rows.append(i)
            cols.append(i*N + k)
            data.append(1)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        for i in range(N):
            rows.append(N + k)
            cols.append(i*N + k)
            data.append(1)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq = sp.csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2*N, total_vars))
    b_eq = np.array(b_eq, dtype=int)
    # Edge constraint matrix
    rows_e=[]
    cols_e=[]
    data_e=[]
    for e_idx,(u,v) in enumerate(edges):
        for k in range(N):
            coeff = k+1
            rows_e.append(e_idx)
            cols_e.append(u*N + k)
            data_e.append(coeff)
            rows_e.append(e_idx)
            cols_e.append(v*N + k)
            data_e.append(coeff)
    A_edge = sp.csr_matrix((data_e, (rows_e, cols_e)), shape=(E, total_vars))
    # binary search S
    low=0
    high=2*N
    best=0
    while low<=high:
        mid=(low+high)//2
        # lower bound for each edge >= mid
        lc = np.full(E, mid, dtype=int)
        uc = np.full(E, np.iinfo(np.int64).max, dtype=int)
        edge_con = LinearConstraint(A_edge, lc, uc)
        eq_con = LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq)
        constraints = [eq_con, edge_con]
        c = np.zeros(total_vars)
        integrality = np.ones(total_vars, dtype=int)
        bounds = Bounds(0,1)
        res = milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, integrality=integrality, bounds=bounds, options={'disp': False})
        if res.success:
            best=mid
            low=mid+1
        else:
            high=mid-1
    return best

print(max_S_grid_milp(4))

def min_edge_sum_grid(grid):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    mins=10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, grid[i,j]+grid[i+1,j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, grid[i,j]+grid[i,j+1])
    return mins

print(min_edge_sum_grid(grid))

[CODE] (turn 17):
def check_feasibility(grid, S):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1<n and grid[i,j]+grid[i+1,j] < S:
                return False
            if j+1<n and grid[i,j]+grid[i,j+1] < S:
                return False
    return True

print(check_feasibility(grid,9))

                return False
            if j+1<n and grid[i,j]+grid[i,j+1] < S:

    return True

[CODE] (turn 1):
import itertools, math

def max_min_adj_sum(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    # adjacency list
    adj={}
    for i,j in cells:
        neigh=[]
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                neigh.append((ni,nj))
        adj[(i,j)]=neigh
    best=-1
    best_perm=None
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        # assign
        lab=dict(zip(cells, perm))
        # compute min edge sum
        min_sum=math.inf
        for (i,j), neigh in adj.items():
            for (ni,nj) in neigh:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj): # count each edge once
                    s=lab[(i,j)]+lab[(ni,nj)]
                    if s<min_sum:
                        min_sum=s
        if min_sum>best:
            best=min_sum
            best_perm=lab.copy()
    return best, best_perm

print(max_min_adj_sum(2,2)[0])

[CODE] (turn 1):
import itertools, math

def max_min_adj_sum(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    # adjacency list
    adj={}
    for i,j in cells:
        neigh=[]
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                neigh.append((ni,nj))
        adj[(i,j)]=neigh
    best=-1
    best_perm=None
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        # assign
        lab=dict(zip(cells, perm))
        # compute min edge sum
        min_sum=math.inf
        for (i,j), neigh in adj.items():
            for (ni,nj) in neigh:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj): # count each edge once
                    s=lab[(i,j)]+lab[(ni,nj)]
                    if s<min_sum:
                        min_sum=s
        if min_sum>best:
            best=min_sum
            best_perm=lab.copy()
    return best, best_perm

print(max_min_adj_sum(2,2)[0])

import itertools, math, numpy as np, sys, time, random, collections, itertools, math, numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_min_adj_sum_ilp(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    cell_index={c:i for i,c in enumerate(cells)}
    # adjacency edges list (unordered)
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                edges.append(((i,j),(ni,nj)))
    # variables x_{c,k} flattened: index = c*N + (k-1)
    num_vars=N*N
    # constraints: each cell assigned exactly one number
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    # each cell sum_k x =1
    for c in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[c*N:(c+1)*N]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[k::N]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # We'll binary search on T
    low=0
    high=2*N # max sum possible = (N)+(N-1) = 2N-1
    best_T=0
    best_sol=None
    while low<=high:
        T=(low+high)//2
        # Build inequality constraints: for each edge, sum_k k x_{c,k} + sum_l l x_{d,l} >= T
        A_ub=[]
        b_ub=[]
        for (c1,c2) in edges:
            row=np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
            idx1=cell_index[c1]
            idx2=cell_index[c2]
            # add coefficients k for variables of c1
            for k in range(N):
                row[idx1*N + k] += (k+1)
                row[idx2*N + k] += (k+1)
            # inequality: -row <= -T  (i.e., row >= T)
            A_ub.append(-row)
            b_ub.append(-T)
        A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
        b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
        # bounds 0-1
        bounds=Bounds(0,1)
        # integer variables
        integrality=np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
        # objective dummy zero
        c=np.zeros(num_vars)
        try:
            res=milp(c=c, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub,
                     bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'presolve':True})
        except Exception as e:
            print("milp error", e)
            return None
        if res.success:
            best_T=T
            best_sol=res.x
            low=T+1
        else:
            high=T-1
    return best_T, best_sol

print("starting 4x4")
best_T, sol = max_min_adj_sum_ilp(4,4)
print("best_T", best_T)

from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_min_adj_sum_ilp(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    cell_index={c:i for i,c in enumerate(cells)}
    # adjacency edges list (unordered)
    edges=[]

            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                edges.append(((i,j),(ni,nj)))
    # variables x_{c,k} flattened: index = c*N + (k-1)
    num_vars=N*N
    # constraints: each cell assigned exactly one number
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]

        row[c*N:(c+1)*N]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
        row[k::N]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # We'll binary search on T
    low=0
    high=2*N # max sum possible = (N)+(N-1) = 2N-1
    best_T=0
    best_sol=None
    while low<=high:
        T=(low+high)//2
        # Build inequality constraints: for each edge, sum_k k x_{c,k} + sum_l l x_{d,l} >= T
        A_ub=[]
        b_ub=[]
        for (c1,c2) in edges:
            row=np.zeros(num_vars, dtype=int)
            idx1=cell_index[c1]
            idx2=cell_index[c2]
            # add coefficients k for variables of c1
            for k in range(N):
                row[idx1*N + k] += (k+1)
                row[idx2*N + k] += (k+1)
            # inequality: -row <= -T  (i.e., row >= T)
            A_ub.append(-row)
            b_ub.append(-T)
        A_ub=np.array(A_ub)
        b_ub=np.array(b_ub)
        # bounds 0-1
        bounds=Bounds(0,1)
        # integer variables

            return None
        if res.success:

            best_sol=res.x
            low=T+1
        else:
            high=T-1

[OUTPUT] (turn 6):
def milp(c, *, integrality=None, bounds=None, constraints=None, options=None):
    r"""
    Mixed-integer linear programming

    Solves problems of the following form:

    .. math::

        \min_x \ & c^T x \\
        \mbox{such that} \ & b_l \leq A x \leq b_u,\\
        & l \leq x \leq u, \\
        & x_i \in \mathbb{Z}, i \in X_i

    where :math:`x` is a vector of decision variables;
    :math:`c`, :math:`b_l`, :math:`b_u`, :math:`l`, and :math:`u` are vectors;
    :math:`A` is a matrix, and :math:`X_i` is the set of indices of
    decision variables that must be integral. (In this context, a
    variable that can assume only integer values is said to be "integral";
    it has an "integrality" constraint.)

    Alternatively, that's:

    minimize::

        c @ x

    such that::

        b_l <= A @ x <= b_u
        l <= x <= u
        Specified elements of x must be integers

    By default, ``l = 0`` and ``u = np.inf`` unless specified with
    ``bounds``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    c : 1D dense array_like
        The coefficients of the linear objective function to be minimized.
        `c` is converted to a double precision array before the problem is
        solved.
    integrality : 1D dense array_like, optional
        Indicates the type of integrality constraint on each decision variable.

        ``0`` : Continuous variable; no integrality constraint.

        ``1`` : Integer variable; decision variable must be an integer
        within `bounds`.

        ``2`` : Semi-continuous variable; decision variable must be within
... (200 lines total)

[CODE] (turn 7):
import numpy as np, itertools, math, sys, time
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_min_adj_sum_ilp_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    cell_index={c:i for i,c in enumerate(cells)}
    # edges (undirected)
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]: # right and down to avoid duplicates
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                edges.append(((i,j),(ni,nj)))
    num_vars=N*N
    # Build equality constraints matrix A_eq x = b_eq
    # each cell assigned exactly one number
    A_eq=[]
    b_eq=[]
    for c in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        row[c*N:(c+1)*N]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    # each number assigned to exactly one cell
    for k in range(N):
        row=np.zeros(num_vars)
        row[k::N]=1
        A_eq.append(row)
        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)
    # bounds 0-1
    bounds=Bounds(0,1)
    integrality=np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
    # binary search T
    low=0
    high=2*N  # max possible sum is (N)+(N-1)=2N-1
    best_T=0
    best_sol=None
    while low<=high:
        T=(low+high)//2
        # Build inequality constraints: sum_k k x_{c,k} + sum_l l x_{d,l} >= T
        # Convert to form b_l <= A x <= b_u
        # We'll set lower bound = T, upper bound = +inf
        rows=[]
        for (c1,c2) in edges:
            row=np.zeros(num_vars)
            idx1=cell_index[c1]
            idx2=cell_index[c2]
            for k in range(N):
                coeff=k+1
                row[idx1*N + k] += coeff
                row[idx2*N + k] += coeff
            rows.append(row)
        A=np.array(rows)
        b_l=np.full(len(edges), T, dtype=float)
        b_u=np.full(len(edges), np.inf, dtype=float)
        constraints=LinearConstraint(A, b_l, b_u)
        # objective dummy zero (minimize 0)
        c=np.zeros(num_vars)
        res=milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, integrality=integrality, bounds=bounds, options={'presolve':True})
        if res.success:
            best_T=T
            best_sol=res.x
            low=T+1
        else:
            high=T-1
    return best_T, best_sol

print("Running 4x4")
best_T, sol = max_min_adj_sum_ilp_grid(4,4)
print("best_T", best_T)

        b_eq.append(1)
    A_eq=np.array(A_eq)
    b_eq=np.array(b_eq)

        b_u=np.full(len(edges), np.inf, dtype=float)

[CODE] (turn 8):
def decode_solution(m,n,sol):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    assign={}
    for idx,c in enumerate(cells):
        # find k where x_{c,k}=1
        for k in range(N):
            if abs(sol[idx*N + k] - 1) < 1e-6:
                assign[c]=k+1
                break
    return assign

assign=decode_solution(4,4,sol)
assign

[CODE] (turn 10):
def max_min_adj_sum_ilp_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    cell_index={c:i for i,c in enumerate(cells)}
    # edges (right, down)
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                edges.append(((i,j),(ni,nj)))
    num_vars=N*N
    # Build equality constraints for cell assignment
    A_cell=np.zeros((N, num_vars))
    for c in range(N):
        A_cell[c, c*N:(c+1)*N]=1
    b_cell=np.ones(N)
    # Build equality constraints for number assignment
    A_num=np.zeros((N, num_vars))
    for k in range(N):
        A_num[k, k::N]=1
    b_num=np.ones(N)
    # Combine equality constraints
    A_eq=np.vstack([A_cell, A_num])
    b_eq=np.concatenate([b_cell, b_num])
    # Edge constraints matrix
    A_edge=np.zeros((len(edges), num_vars))
    for idx,(c1,c2) in enumerate(edges):
        i1=cell_index[c1]
        i2=cell_index[c2]
        for k in range(N):
            coeff=k+1
            A_edge[idx, i1*N + k] += coeff
            A_edge[idx, i2*N + k] += coeff
    # binary search T
    low=0
    high=2*N  # max possible sum
    best_T=0
    best_sol=None
    while low<=high:
        T=(low+high)//2
        # constraints list
        constraints=[]
        constraints.append(LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq))  # equality
        constraints.append(LinearConstraint(A_edge, np.full(len(edges), T, dtype=float), np.full(len(edges), np.inf, dtype=float)))
        bounds=Bounds(0,1)
        integrality=np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
        c=np.zeros(num_vars)
        res=milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, integrality=integrality, bounds=bounds, options={'presolve':True})
        if res.success:
            best_T=T
            best_sol=res.x
            low=T+1
        else:
            high=T-1
    return best_T, best_sol

best_T, sol = max_min_adj_sum_ilp_grid(4,4)
print("best_T", best_T)

def min_adj_sum(m,n,assign):
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    min_sum=10**9
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                s=assign[(i,j)]+assign[(ni,nj)]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
    return min_sum

print(min_adj_sum(4,4,assign))

def min_adj_sum(m,n,assign):
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    min_sum=10**9
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                s=assign[(i,j)]+assign[(ni,nj)]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
    return min_sum

import random, math, itertools, collections, numpy as np, time, sys, math, copy

def min_adj_sum_grid(m,n,grid):
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1<m:
                s=grid[i][j]+grid[i+1][j]
                if s<min_sum: min_sum=s
            if j+1<n:
                s=grid[i][j]+grid[i][j+1]
                if s<min_sum: min_sum=s
    return min_sum

def random_grid(m,n):
    N=m*n
    nums=list(range(1,N+1))
    random.shuffle(nums)
    grid=[[0]*n for _ in range(m)]
    idx=0
    for i in range(m):
        for j in range(n):
            grid[i][j]=nums[idx]; idx+=1
    return grid

def improve_grid(m,n,iterations=200000):
    grid=random_grid(m,n)
    best=grid
    best_score=min_adj_sum_grid(m,n,grid)
    for it in range(iterations):
        # pick two cells to swap
        i1,j1=random.randrange(m), random.randrange(n)
        i2,j2=random.randrange(m), random.randrange(n)
        if i1==i2 and j1==j2: continue
        # swap
        grid[i1][j1],grid[i2][j2]=grid[i2][j2],grid[i1][j1]
        score=min_adj_sum_grid(m,n,grid)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best=[row[:] for row in grid]
        # accept if not worse? we can use simulated annealing
        # simple hill climbing: keep swap only if score >= current
        # else revert
        # Let's compute current score before swap
        # Actually we need current score variable
        # We'll compute current score before swap
        # Let's restructure

[CODE] (turn 15):
def hill_climb(m,n,steps=200000):
    grid=random_grid(m,n)
    cur_score=min_adj_sum_grid(m,n,grid)
    for step in range(steps):
        i1,j1=random.randrange(m), random.randrange(n)
        i2,j2=random.randrange(m), random.randrange(n)
        if i1==i2 and j1==j2:
            continue
        # compute affected edges before swap to compute new min efficiently? We'll just recompute full.
        # swap
        grid[i1][j1],grid[i2][j2]=grid[i2][j2],grid[i1][j1]
        new_score=min_adj_sum_grid(m,n,grid)
        if new_score>=cur_score:
            cur_score=new_score
        else:
            # revert
            grid[i1][j1],grid[i2][j2]=grid[i2][j2],grid[i1][j1]
    return cur_score, grid

def find_best(m,n,tries=20,steps=200000):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for t in range(tries):
        score,grid=hill_climb(m,n,steps)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=[row[:] for row in grid]
            print("new best",best_score)
    return best_score,best_grid

best6,grid6=find_best(6,6,tries=10,steps=200000)
print("best6",best6)

def hill_climb(m,n,steps=200000):
    grid=random_grid(m,n)
    cur_score=min_adj_sum_grid(m,n,grid)
    for step in range(steps):
        i1,j1=random.randrange(m), random.randrange(n)
        i2,j2=random.randrange(m), random.randrange(n)
        if i1==i2 and j1==j2:
            continue
        # compute affected edges before swap to compute new min efficiently? We'll just recompute full.
        # swap
        grid[i1][j1],grid[i2][j2]=grid[i2][j2],grid[i1][j1]
        new_score=min_adj_sum_grid(m,n,grid)
        if new_score>=cur_score:
            cur_score=new_score
        else:
            # revert
            grid[i1][j1],grid[i2][j2]=grid[i2][j2],grid[i1][j1]
    return cur_score, grid

def find_best(m,n,tries=20,steps=200000):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for t in range(tries):
        score,grid=hill_climb(m,n,steps)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=[row[:] for row in grid]
            print("new best",best_score)
    return best_score,best_grid

[CODE] (turn 17):
def max_min_adj_sum_ilp_grid_fast(m,n, time_limit=60):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    cell_index={c:i for i,c in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                edges.append(((i,j),(ni,nj)))
    num_vars=N*N
    # equality constraints
    A_eq=np.zeros((2*N, num_vars))
    b_eq=np.ones(2*N)
    # cell constraints
    for c in range(N):
        A_eq[c, c*N:(c+1)*N]=1
    # number constraints
    for k in range(N):
        A_eq[N+k, k::N]=1
    # edge matrix
    A_edge=np.zeros((len(edges), num_vars))
    for idx,(c1,c2) in enumerate(edges):
        i1=cell_index[c1]
        i2=cell_index[c2]
        for k in range(N):
            coeff=k+1
            A_edge[idx, i1*N + k] += coeff
            A_edge[idx, i2*N + k] += coeff
    low=0
    high=2*N
    best_T=0
    best_sol=None
    while low<=high:
        T=(low+high)//2
        constraints=[LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq),
                     LinearConstraint(A_edge, np.full(len(edges), T, dtype=float), np.full(len(edges), np.inf, dtype=float))]
        bounds=Bounds(0,1)
        integrality=np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
        c=np.zeros(num_vars)
        res=milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, integrality=integrality, bounds=bounds, options={'presolve':True, 'time_limit':time_limit})
        if res.success:
            best_T=T
            best_sol=res.x
            low=T+1
        else:
            high=T-1
    return best_T, best_sol

best_T5, sol5 = max_min_adj_sum_ilp_grid_fast(5,5, time_limit=30)
print("best_T5", best_T5)

        i1=cell_index[c1]
        i2=cell_index[c2]
        for k in range(N):
            coeff=k+1
            A_edge[idx, i1*N + k] += coeff
            A_edge[idx, i2*N + k] += coeff
    low=0
    high=2*N
    best_T=0
    best_sol=None
    while low<=high:
        T=(low+high)//2
        constraints=[LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq),
                     LinearConstraint(A_edge, np.full(len(edges), T, dtype=float), np.full(len(edges), np.inf, dtype=float))]

[CODE] (turn 20):
def max_min_adj_sum_bruteforce(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    adj=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                adj.append(((i,j),(ni,nj)))
    best=0
    best_assign=None
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        assign=dict(zip(cells, perm))
        min_sum=10**9
        for (c1,c2) in adj:
            s=assign[c1]+assign[c2]
            if s<min_sum:
                min_sum=s
                if min_sum<=best: # prune
                    break
        else:
            if min_sum>best:
                best=min_sum
                best_assign=assign.copy()
    return best, best_assign

print(max_min_adj_sum_bruteforce(2,3)[0])

        for (c1,c2) in adj:

                if min_sum<=best: # prune
                    break
        else:
            if min_sum>best:
                best=min_sum

def max_min_adj_sum_ilp(m,n, time_limit=30):
    import numpy as np
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    cell_index={c:i for i,c in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                edges.append(((i,j),(ni,nj)))
    num_vars=N*N
    # equality constraints
    A_eq=np.zeros((2*N, num_vars))
    b_eq=np.ones(2*N)
    # cell constraints
    for c in range(N):
        A_eq[c, c*N:(c+1)*N]=1
    # number constraints
    for k in range(N):
        A_eq[N+k, k::N]=1
    # edge matrix
    A_edge=np.zeros((len(edges), num_vars))
    for idx,(c1,c2) in enumerate(edges):
        i1=cell_index[c1]
        i2=cell_index[c2]
        for k in range(N):
            coeff=k+1
            A_edge[idx, i1*N + k] += coeff
            A_edge[idx, i2*N + k] += coeff
    low=0
    high=2*N
    best_T=0
    best_sol=None
    while low<=high:
        T=(low+high)//2
        constraints=[LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq),
                     LinearConstraint(A_edge, np.full(len(edges), T, dtype=float), np.full(len(edges), np.inf, dtype=float))]
        bounds=Bounds(0,1)
        integrality=np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
        c=np.zeros(num_vars)
        res=milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, integrality=integrality, bounds=bounds, options={'presolve':True, 'time_limit':time_limit})
        if res.success:
            best_T=T
            best_sol=res.x
            low=T+1
        else:
            high=T-1
    return best_T, best_sol

best_T34, sol34 = max_min_adj_sum_ilp(3,4, time_limit=30)
print(best_T34)

def max_min_adj_sum_ilp(m,n, time_limit=30):
    import numpy as np
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    N=m*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    cell_index={c:i for i,c in enumerate(cells)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(0,1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                edges.append(((i,j),(ni,nj)))
    num_vars=N*N
    # equality constraints
    A_eq=np.zeros((2*N, num_vars))
    b_eq=np.ones(2*N)
    # cell constraints
    for c in range(N):
        A_eq[c, c*N:(c+1)*N]=1
    # number constraints
    for k in range(N):
        A_eq[N+k, k::N]=1
    # edge matrix
    A_edge=np.zeros((len(edges), num_vars))
    for idx,(c1,c2) in enumerate(edges):
        i1=cell_index[c1]
        i2=cell_index[c2]
        for k in range(N):
            coeff=k+1
            A_edge[idx, i1*N + k] += coeff
            A_edge[idx, i2*N + k] += coeff
    low=0
    high=2*N
    best_T=0
    best_sol=None
    while low<=high:
        T=(low+high)//2
        constraints=[LinearConstraint(A_eq, b_eq, b_eq),
                     LinearConstraint(A_edge, np.full(len(edges), T, dtype=float), np.full(len(edges), np.inf, dtype=float))]
        bounds=Bounds(0,1)
        integrality=np.ones(num_vars, dtype=int)
        c=np.zeros(num_vars)
        res=milp(c=c, constraints=constraints, integrality=integrality, bounds=bounds, options={'presolve':True, 'time_limit':time_limit})
        if res.success:
            best_T=T
            best_sol=res.x
            low=T+1
        else:
            high=T-1
    return best_T, best_sol

best_T34, sol34 = max_min_adj_sum_ilp(3,4, time_limit=30)

import math

total_vertices = 3600
deg_counts = {4:3364, 3:232, 2:4}
# assign numbers
# create list of numbers 1..3600
numbers = list(range(1, 3601))
# assign largest to degree 4
# We'll compute sum deg*value
# We'll assign highest numbers to degree 4, then next to degree 3, then lowest to degree 2
# So sort numbers descending
nums_desc = sorted(numbers, reverse=True)
idx = 0
total_edge_sum_max = 0
for deg, cnt in [(4,3364),(3,232),(2,4)]:
    for i in range(cnt):
        val = nums_desc[idx]
        total_edge_sum_max += deg * val
        idx += 1

# assign numbers: we need to assign largest numbers to interior cells (i=1..58, j=1..58)

# We'll assign smallest numbers to corners and edges, largest to interior.
# Determine positions categories
corners = [(0,0),(0,n-1),(n-1,0),(n-1,n-1)]
edge_positions = []
for i in range(n):
    for j in range(n):
        if (i,j) in corners:
            continue
        if i==0 or i==n-1 or j==0 or j==n-1:
            edge_positions.append((i,j))
# interior positions
interior_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(1,n-1) for j in range(1,n-1)]

[CODE] (turn 5):
import random, itertools, math, numpy as np

def random_assignment():
    n=60
    grid = np.empty((n,n), dtype=int)
    # color pattern: (i+j)%2 == 0 -> black, else white
    black_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0]
    white_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==1]
    # assign numbers
    numbers = list(range(1,3601))
    random.shuffle(numbers)
    # assign smallest 1800 to black, largest 1800 to white
    black_vals = sorted(numbers[:1800])  # smallest
    white_vals = sorted(numbers[1800:])  # larger
    # assign randomly within each color
    random.shuffle(black_vals)
    random.shuffle(white_vals)
    for pos,val in zip(black_positions, black_vals):
        grid[pos]=val
    for pos,val in zip(white_positions, white_vals):
        grid[pos]=val
    return grid

def min_adjacent_sum_grid(grid):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=grid[i,j]
            if i+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i+1,j]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
            if j+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i,j+1]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
    return min_sum

# test a few random assignments
mins=[]
for _ in range(5):
    g=random_assignment()
    mins.append(min_adjacent_sum_grid(g))
mins

import random, itertools, math, numpy as np

def random_assignment():
    n=60
    grid = np.empty((n,n), dtype=int)
    # color pattern: (i+j)%2 == 0 -> black, else white
    black_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0]
    white_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==1]
    # assign numbers
    numbers = list(range(1,3601))
    random.shuffle(numbers)
    # assign smallest 1800 to black, largest 1800 to white
    black_vals = sorted(numbers[:1800])  # smallest
    white_vals = sorted(numbers[1800:])  # larger
    # assign randomly within each color
    random.shuffle(black_vals)
    random.shuffle(white_vals)
    for pos,val in zip(black_positions, black_vals):
        grid[pos]=val
    for pos,val in zip(white_positions, white_vals):
        grid[pos]=val
    return grid

def min_adjacent_sum_grid(grid):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=grid[i,j]
            if i+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i+1,j]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
            if j+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i,j+1]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
    return min_sum

[CODE] (turn 6):
def construct_arrangement():
    n=60
    grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
    # Determine color: black if (i+j)%2==0
    black_positions = []
    white_positions = []
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                black_positions.append((i,j))
            else:
                white_positions.append((i,j))
    # categorize by degree
    def degree(pos):
        i,j = pos
        deg=0
        if i>0: deg+=1
        if i<n-1: deg+=1
        if j>0: deg+=1
        if j<n-1: deg+=1
        return deg
    # sort black positions by degree descending (interior first) to assign smallest numbers there
    black_positions_sorted = sorted(black_positions, key=lambda p: degree(p), reverse=True)
    # sort white positions by degree descending to assign largest numbers there
    white_positions_sorted = sorted(white_positions, key=lambda p: degree(p), reverse=True)
    # assign numbers
    numbers = list(range(1,3601))
    # For black: assign smallest numbers to interior (high degree)
    # We'll assign numbers in increasing order to black_positions_sorted
    for idx,pos in enumerate(black_positions_sorted):
        grid[pos] = numbers[idx]  # smallest first
    # For white: assign largest numbers to interior (high degree)
    # assign numbers in decreasing order to white_positions_sorted
    for idx,pos in enumerate(white_positions_sorted):
        grid[pos] = numbers[-1-idx]  # largest first
    return grid

grid = construct_arrangement()
min_adjacent_sum_grid(grid)

def construct_arrangement():
    n=60
    grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
    # Determine color: black if (i+j)%2==0
    black_positions = []
    white_positions = []
    for i in range(n):

                black_positions.append((i,j))
            else:
                white_positions.append((i,j))
    # categorize by degree

        i,j = pos
        deg=0
        if i>0: deg+=1
        if i<n-1: deg+=1
        if j>0: deg+=1
        if j<n-1: deg+=1
        return deg
    # sort black positions by degree descending (interior first) to assign smallest numbers there
    black_positions_sorted = sorted(black_positions, key=lambda p: degree(p), reverse=True)
    # sort white positions by degree descending to assign largest numbers there
    white_positions_sorted = sorted(white_positions, key=lambda p: degree(p), reverse=True)
    # assign numbers
    numbers = list(range(1,3601))
    # For black: assign smallest numbers to interior (high degree)
    # We'll assign numbers in increasing order to black_positions_sorted
    for idx,pos in enumerate(black_positions_sorted):
        grid[pos] = numbers[idx]  # smallest first
    # For white: assign largest numbers to interior (high degree)
    # assign numbers in decreasing order to white_positions_sorted
    for idx,pos in enumerate(white_positions_sorted):

    return grid

def count_black_by_degree():
    n=60
    cnt = {}
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                deg = (i>0)+(i<n-1)+(j>0)+(j<n-1)
                cnt[deg]=cnt.get(deg,0)+1
    return cnt

def find_min_edge(grid):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    min_sum=10**9
    min_edge=None
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=grid[i,j]
            if i+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i+1,j]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
                    min_edge=((i,j),(i+1,j))
            if j+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i,j+1]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
                    min_edge=((i,j),(i,j+1))
    return min_sum, min_edge

min_sum, edge = find_min_edge(grid)
min_sum, edge

import random, time, math, numpy as np

def initial_grid():
    n=60
    grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
    black_positions = []
    white_positions = []
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                black_positions.append((i,j))
            else:
                white_positions.append((i,j))
    # degree function
    def deg(pos):
        i,j=pos
        d=0
        if i>0: d+=1
        if i<n-1: d+=1
        if j>0: d+=1
        if j<n-1: d+=1
        return d
    # sort black by degree descending (interior first)
    black_positions.sort(key=deg, reverse=True)
    # sort white by degree descending
    white_positions.sort(key=deg, reverse=True)
    # assign numbers
    numbers = list(range(1,3601))
    # assign smallest to black (in order)
    for idx,pos in enumerate(black_positions):
        grid[pos]=numbers[idx]
    # assign largest to white (in order)
    for idx,pos in enumerate(white_positions):
        grid[pos]=numbers[-1-idx]
    return grid

def min_adjacent_sum(grid):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            v=grid[i,j]
            if i+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i+1,j]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
            if j+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i,j+1]
                if s<min_sum:
                    min_sum=s
    return min_sum

grid = initial_grid()
print("initial min sum", min_adjacent_sum(grid))

[CODE] (turn 12):
def simulated_anneal(grid, max_iter=200000, start_temp=1000, end_temp=1):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    best_grid = grid.copy()
    best_score = min_adjacent_sum(grid)
    cur_grid = grid.copy()
    cur_score = best_score
    # precompute neighbor list for each cell to speed up incremental update? We'll compute full each time for simplicity.
    for it in range(max_iter):
        # temperature schedule linear
        T = start_temp + (end_temp - start_temp) * it / max_iter
        # pick two random cells (maybe any)
        i1,j1 = random.randint(0,n-1), random.randint(0,n-1)
        i2,j2 = random.randint(0,n-1), random.randint(0,n-1)
        if (i1,j1)==(i2,j2):
            continue
        # swap
        cur_grid[i1,j1], cur_grid[i2,j2] = cur_grid[i2,j2], cur_grid[i1,j1]
        new_score = min_adjacent_sum(cur_grid)
        delta = new_score - cur_score
        if delta > 0 or random.random() < math.exp(delta / T if T>0 else 0):
            cur_score = new_score
            if new_score > best_score:
                best_score = new_score
                best_grid = cur_grid.copy()
                # print progress
                if best_score % 10 == 0:
                    print("iter", it, "best", best_score)
        else:
            # revert swap
            cur_grid[i1,j1], cur_grid[i2,j2] = cur_grid[i2,j2], cur_grid[i1,j1]
    return best_grid, best_score

best_grid, best_score = simulated_anneal(grid, max_iter=20000, start_temp=1000, end_temp=1)
print("best_score", best_score)

[CODE] (turn 12):
def simulated_anneal(grid, max_iter=200000, start_temp=1000, end_temp=1):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    best_grid = grid.copy()
    best_score = min_adjacent_sum(grid)
    cur_grid = grid.copy()
    cur_score = best_score
    # precompute neighbor list for each cell to speed up incremental update? We'll compute full each time for simplicity.
    for it in range(max_iter):
        # temperature schedule linear
        T = start_temp + (end_temp - start_temp) * it / max_iter
        # pick two random cells (maybe any)
        i1,j1 = random.randint(0,n-1), random.randint(0,n-1)
        i2,j2 = random.randint(0,n-1), random.randint(0,n-1)
        if (i1,j1)==(i2,j2):
            continue
        # swap
        cur_grid[i1,j1], cur_grid[i2,j2] = cur_grid[i2,j2], cur_grid[i1,j1]
        new_score = min_adjacent_sum(cur_grid)
        delta = new_score - cur_score
        if delta > 0 or random.random() < math.exp(delta / T if T>0 else 0):
            cur_score = new_score
            if new_score > best_score:
                best_score = new_score
                best_grid = cur_grid.copy()
                # print progress
                if best_score % 10 == 0:
                    print("iter", it, "best", best_score)
        else:
            # revert swap
            cur_grid[i1,j1], cur_grid[i2,j2] = cur_grid[i2,j2], cur_grid[i1,j1]
    return best_grid, best_score

best_grid, best_score = simulated_anneal(grid, max_iter=20000, start_temp=1000, end_temp=1)

[CODE] (turn 13):
def construct_custom():
    n=60
    grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
    # Determine colors
    black = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0]
    white = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==1]
    # Identify black corners (degree 2)
    black_corners = [(0,0),(n-1,n-1)]  # both even parity
    # Identify white edge squares adjacent to black corners
    # For (0,0) neighbors: (0,1) and (1,0)
    # For (n-1,n-1) neighbors: (n-1,n-2) and (n-2,n-1)
    white_adj_to_black_corners = [(0,1),(1,0),(n-1,n-2),(n-2,n-1)]
    # Ensure they are white squares (they should be)
    # Assign smallest numbers to black corners
    grid[0,0]=1
    grid[n-1,n-1]=2
    # Assign largest numbers to those white neighbors
    high_vals = [3600,3599,3598,3597]
    for pos,val in zip(white_adj_to_black_corners, high_vals):
        grid[pos]=val
    # Now we need to assign remaining numbers to remaining cells.
    # We'll use degree-based assignment for rest.
    # Remove already assigned positions from lists
    assigned = set(black_corners + white_adj_to_black_corners)
    remaining_black = [pos for pos in black if pos not in assigned]
    remaining_white = [pos for pos in white if pos not in assigned]
    # Sort remaining black by degree descending (interior first)
    def deg(pos):
        i,j=pos
        d=0
        if i>0: d+=1
        if i<n-1: d+=1
        if j>0: d+=1
        if j<n-1: d+=1
        return d
    remaining_black.sort(key=deg, reverse=True)
    remaining_white.sort(key=deg, reverse=True)
    # Determine which numbers are left
    used_vals = {grid[pos] for pos in assigned}
    remaining_vals = [v for v in range(1,3601) if v not in used_vals]
    # We'll assign smallest remaining to black, largest remaining to white (as before)
    # assign to black
    for idx,pos in enumerate(remaining_black):
        grid[pos]=remaining_vals[idx]
    # assign to white (largest)
    for idx,pos in enumerate(remaining_white):
        grid[pos]=remaining_vals[-1-idx]
    return grid

grid2 = construct_custom()
print(min_adjacent_sum_grid(grid2))

[CODE] (turn 13):
def construct_custom():
    n=60
    grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
    # Determine colors
    black = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0]
    white = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==1]
    # Identify black corners (degree 2)
    black_corners = [(0,0),(n-1,n-1)]  # both even parity
    # Identify white edge squares adjacent to black corners
    # For (0,0) neighbors: (0,1) and (1,0)
    # For (n-1,n-1) neighbors: (n-1,n-2) and (n-2,n-1)
    white_adj_to_black_corners = [(0,1),(1,0),(n-1,n-2),(n-2,n-1)]
    # Ensure they are white squares (they should be)
    # Assign smallest numbers to black corners
    grid[0,0]=1
    grid[n-1,n-1]=2
    # Assign largest numbers to those white neighbors
    high_vals = [3600,3599,3598,3597]
    for pos,val in zip(white_adj_to_black_corners, high_vals):
        grid[pos]=val
    # Now we need to assign remaining numbers to remaining cells.
    # We'll use degree-based assignment for rest.
    # Remove already assigned positions from lists
    assigned = set(black_corners + white_adj_to_black_corners)
    remaining_black = [pos for pos in black if pos not in assigned]
    remaining_white = [pos for pos in white if pos not in assigned]
    # Sort remaining black by degree descending (interior first)
    def deg(pos):
        i,j=pos
        d=0
        if i>0: d+=1
        if i<n-1: d+=1
        if j>0: d+=1
        if j<n-1: d+=1
        return d
    remaining_black.sort(key=deg, reverse=True)
    remaining_white.sort(key=deg, reverse=True)
    # Determine which numbers are left
    used_vals = {grid[pos] for pos in assigned}
    remaining_vals = [v for v in range(1,3601) if v not in used_vals]
    # We'll assign smallest remaining to black, largest remaining to white (as before)
    # assign to black
    for idx,pos in enumerate(remaining_black):
        grid[pos]=remaining_vals[idx]
    # assign to white (largest)
    for idx,pos in enumerate(remaining_white):
        grid[pos]=remaining_vals[-1-idx]
    return grid

grid2 = construct_custom()
print(min_adjacent_sum_grid(grid2))

def construct_degree_based():
    n=60
    grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
    # color sets
    black_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0]
    white_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==1]
    # degree function
    def deg(pos):
        i,j=pos
        d=0
        if i>0: d+=1
        if i<n-1: d+=1
        if j>0: d+=1
        if j<n-1: d+=1
        return d
    # sort black by degree ascending (low degree first)
    black_positions.sort(key=deg)  # ascending
    # sort white by degree descending (high degree first)
    white_positions.sort(key=deg, reverse=True)
    # assign black numbers 1..1800 to black positions in order
    black_vals = list(range(1,1801))
    for pos,val in zip(black_positions, black_vals):
        grid[pos]=val
    # assign white numbers 1801..3600 to white positions in order (largest to interior)
    white_vals = list(range(1801,3601))
    # we want highest numbers to interior, so assign in descending order to sorted list
    white_vals_desc = white_vals[::-1]
    for pos,val in zip(white_positions, white_vals_desc):
        grid[pos]=val
    return grid

grid3 = construct_degree_based()
print(min_adjacent_sum_grid(grid3))

def construct_balanced():
    n=60
    grid = np.zeros((n,n), dtype=int)
    black_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0]
    white_positions = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==1]
    def deg(pos):
        i,j=pos
        d=0
        if i>0: d+=1
        if i<n-1: d+=1
        if j>0: d+=1
        if j<n-1: d+=1
        return d
    # sort black by degree ascending
    black_positions.sort(key=deg)  # low degree first
    # sort white by degree descending
    white_positions.sort(key=deg, reverse=True)  # high degree first
    # assign black numbers: smallest to low-degree blacks
    black_vals = list(range(1,1801))
    for pos,val in zip(black_positions, black_vals):

    for pos,val in zip(white_positions, white_vals_desc):

def count_deep_black():

def improve_grid(grid, iterations=50000):
    n=grid.shape[0]
    # precompute adjacency list
    adj = {}
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            neighbors = []
            if i>0: neighbors.append((i-1,j))
            if i<n-1: neighbors.append((i+1,j))
            if j>0: neighbors.append((i,j-1))
            if j<n-1: neighbors.append((i,j+1))
            adj[(i,j)] = neighbors
    cur_grid = grid.copy()
    cur_min = min_adjacent_sum(cur_grid)
    for it in range(iterations):
        # find edges with current minimal sum
        # we can sample random edge and compute its sum
        i = random.randint(0,n-1)
        j = random.randint(0,n-1)
        # pick random neighbor
        nb = random.choice(adj[(i,j)])
        s = cur_grid[i,j] + cur_grid[nb]
        # if this edge sum is equal to current min, try to improve
        if s == cur_min:
            # attempt swap value of (i,j) with another cell of same color that has larger value
            color = (i+j)%2
            # find candidate cell of same color with larger value
            # sample random other cell of same color
            for _ in range(10):
                ii = random.randint(0,n-1)
                jj = random.randint(0,n-1)
                if (ii+jj)%2 != color:
                    continue
                if cur_grid[ii,jj] > cur_grid[i,j]:
                    # swap
                    cur_grid[i,j], cur_grid[ii,jj] = cur_grid[ii,jj], cur_grid[i,j]
                    new_min = min_adjacent_sum(cur_grid)
                    if new_min >= cur_min:
                        cur_min = new_min
                    else:
                        # revert
                        cur_grid[i,j], cur_grid[ii,jj] = cur_grid[ii,jj], cur_grid[i,j]
                    break
    return cur_grid, cur_min

grid5, min5 = improve_grid(grid3, iterations=20000)
print(min5)
