import random, math, itertools, sys, collections, numpy as np, time, itertools, math, sys, collections, random, numpy as np
def generate_grid():
    n=60
    # create list of cells with degree
    cells=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            deg=4
            if i==0 or i==n-1: deg-=1
            if j==0 or j==n-1: deg-=1
            cells.append(((i,j),deg))
    # sort cells by degree descending
    cells.sort(key=lambda x: -x[1])
    # assign numbers descending
    labels={}
    num=3600
    for (pos,deg) in cells:
        labels[pos]=num
        num-=1
    # compute minimal edge sum
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            cur=labels[(i,j)]
            if i+1<n:
                s=cur+labels[(i+1,j)]
                min_sum=min(min_sum,s)
            if j+1<n:
                s=cur+labels[(i,j+1)]
                min_sum=min(min_sum,s)
    return min_sum

mins=[]
for _ in range(5):
    mins.append(generate_grid())
mins

    degs=[]

        for j in range(n):
            deg=4
            if i==0 or i==n-1: deg-=1

[CODE] (turn 7):
def feasible_S(S):
    # check condition for all t from 0..3600
    for t in range(0,3601):
        k = 3601 - S + t
        if k < 0:
            # then RHS = 0, need D_low(t) <= 0, only possible if t=0
            if t>0:
                return False
        else:
            if k > 3600:
                k = 3600
            if cum_low[t] > cum_high[k]:
                return False
    return True

feasible_S(3601)

[CODE] (turn 8):
def feasible_S_capacity(S):
    # compute D(T) and C(T) for T from 1..3600
    # D(T) = sum of degrees of low numbers a where a <= S - T
    # low numbers are assigned to smallest-degree cells
    # So for k = min(max(0, S - T), 3600) low numbers
    for T in range(1, 3601):
        k = S - T
        if k <= 0:
            demand = 0
        else:
            if k > 3600:
                k = 3600
            demand = cum_low[k]  # sum of smallest k degrees
        # capacity: number of cells with label >= T
        m = 3600 - T + 1
        if m < 0:
            capacity = 0
        else:
            if m > 3600:
                m = 3600
            capacity = cum_high[m]  # sum of largest m degrees
        if demand > capacity:
            return False
    return True

# test for some S
for S in range(3500, 3602):
    print(S, feasible_S_capacity(S))

[CODE] (turn 8):
def feasible_S_capacity(S):
    # compute D(T) and C(T) for T from 1..3600
    # D(T) = sum of degrees of low numbers a where a <= S - T
    # low numbers are assigned to smallest-degree cells
    # So for k = min(max(0, S - T), 3600) low numbers
    for T in range(1, 3601):
        k = S - T
        if k <= 0:
            demand = 0
        else:
            if k > 3600:
                k = 3600
            demand = cum_low[k]  # sum of smallest k degrees
        # capacity: number of cells with label >= T
        m = 3600 - T + 1
        if m < 0:
            capacity = 0
        else:
            if m > 3600:
                m = 3600
            capacity = cum_high[m]  # sum of largest m degrees
        if demand > capacity:
            return False
    return True

# test for some S
for S in range(3500, 3602):
    print(S, feasible_S_capacity(S))

[CODE] (turn 9):
import itertools

def max_S_grid(n):
    # n x n grid
    N=n*n
    cells=[(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n)]
    # adjacency list
    adj={}
    for i,j in cells:
        neigh=[]
        if i>0: neigh.append((i-1,j))
        if i<n-1: neigh.append((i+1,j))
        if j>0: neigh.append((i,j-1))
        if j<n-1: neigh.append((i,j+1))
        adj[(i,j)]=neigh
    maxS=0
    best=None
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        # assign
        label={}
        for idx,cell in enumerate(cells):
            label[cell]=perm[idx]
        # compute min sum
        min_sum=10**9
        for cell in cells:
            for nb in adj[cell]:
                if cell < nb: # avoid double count
                    s=label[cell]+label[nb]
                    if s<min_sum:
                        min_sum=s
        if min_sum>maxS:
            maxS=min_sum
            best=perm
    return maxS

print(max_S_grid(2))

        if j<n-1: neigh.append((i,j+1))
        adj[(i,j)]=neigh
    maxS=0
    best=None

[CODE] (turn 11):
import random, math, time, itertools, numpy as np

def min_adj_sum(grid):
    n = grid.shape[0]
    min_sum = 10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if i+1<n:
                s = grid[i,j] + grid[i+1,j]
                if s < min_sum: min_sum = s
            if j+1<n:
                s = grid[i,j] + grid[i,j+1]
                if s < min_sum: min_sum = s
    return min_sum

def random_initial(n):
    arr = np.arange(1, n*n+1)
    np.random.shuffle(arr)
    return arr.reshape((n,n))

def hill_climb(n, iterations=20000):
    grid = random_initial(n)
    best = grid.copy()
    best_score = min_adj_sum(grid)
    for it in range(iterations):
        # propose swap two cells
        i1,j1 = divmod(random.randrange(n*n), n)
        i2,j2 = divmod(random.randrange(n*n), n)
        if i1==i2 and j1==j2: continue
        # swap
        grid[i1,j1], grid[i2,j2] = grid[i2,j2], grid[i1,j1]
        score = min_adj_sum(grid)
        if score > best_score:
            best_score = score
            best = grid.copy()
        # accept if not worse? simple hill climb: keep if not worse else revert
        if score >= best_score:
            pass
        else:
            # revert
            grid[i1,j1], grid[i2,j2] = grid[i2,j2], grid[i1,j1]
    return best_score, best

best_score, best_grid = hill_climb(4, iterations=50000)
best_score

[CODE] (turn 12):
def upper_bound_n(n):
    N=n*n
    # compute degrees list
    degs=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            deg=4
            if i==0 or i==n-1: deg-=1
            if j==0 or j==n-1: deg-=1
            degs.append(deg)
    # sort descending for max sum
    degs.sort(reverse=True)
    # assign largest numbers to highest degrees
    total=0
    label=N
    for d in degs:
        total+=d*label
        label-=1
    edges = n*(n-1)*2
    return total//edges

print(upper_bound_n(4))

def upper_bound_n(n):
    N=n*n
    # compute degrees list
    degs=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            deg=4
            if i==0 or i==n-1: deg-=1
            if j==0 or j==n-1: deg-=1
            degs.append(deg)
    # sort descending for max sum
    degs.sort(reverse=True)
    # assign largest numbers to highest degrees
    total=0
    label=N
    for d in degs:
        total+=d*label
        label-=1
    edges = n*(n-1)*2
    return total//edges

def simulated_annealing(n, steps=200000, start_temp=5.0, end_temp=0.1):
    grid = random_initial(n)
    best = grid.copy()
    best_score = min_adj_sum(grid)
    temp = start_temp
    for step in range(steps):
        # linear cooling
        temp = start_temp + (end_temp - start_temp) * (step/steps)
        # propose swap
        i1,j1 = divmod(random.randrange(n*n), n)
        i2,j2 = divmod(random.randrange(n*n), n)
        if i1==i2 and j1==j2: continue
        # compute delta in min sum efficiently? just recompute after swap
        grid[i1,j1], grid[i2,j2] = grid[i2,j2], grid[i1,j1]
        score = min_adj_sum(grid)
        # accept if better or with probability
        if score > best_score:
            best_score = score
            best = grid.copy()
        # Metropolis criterion: accept if score >= current_score or random < exp((score-current)/temp)
        # Need current score before swap
        # Let's compute current score before swap for acceptance
        # We'll compute again after swap? Simpler: accept if score >= current_score else with prob
        # compute current score (before swap) by recomputing? Not efficient but okay for small n
        # Actually we can store previous score
        # Let's just accept if score >= current_score else revert with probability
        # We'll compute current_score as min_adj_sum after revert? Not needed.
        # For simplicity, accept if score >= best_score else revert
        if score < best_score and random.random() > math.exp((score - best_score)/temp):
            # revert
            grid[i1,j1], grid[i2,j2] = grid[i2,j2], grid[i1,j1]
    return best_score, best

best_score, best_grid = simulated_annealing(4, steps=200000)
best_score

    grid = random_initial(n)
    best = grid.copy()
    best_score = min_adj_sum(grid)
    temp = start_temp

        # accept if better or with probability
        if score > best_score:
            best_score = score
            best = grid.copy()

        # Need current score before swap
        # Let's compute current score before swap for acceptance

        # Actually we can store previous score
        # Let's just accept if score >= current_score else revert with probability
        # We'll compute current_score as min_adj_sum after revert? Not needed.

[CODE] (turn 14):
def max_min_sum_heuristic(n, trials=5):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for _ in range(trials):
        score,grid = simulated_annealing(n, steps=200000)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=grid
    return best_score

print("5x5 approx", max_min_sum_heuristic(5, trials=3))

[CODE] (turn 16):
def assign_by_degree(n=60):
    cells=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            deg=4
            if i==0 or i==n-1: deg-=1
            if j==0 or j==n-1: deg-=1
            cells.append(((i,j),deg))
    # sort cells by degree descending
    cells.sort(key=lambda x: -x[1])
    # assign numbers descending
    labels={}
    num= n*n
    for (pos,deg) in cells:
        labels[pos]=num
        num-=1
    # compute min edge sum
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            cur=labels[(i,j)]
            if i+1<n:
                s=cur+labels[(i+1,j)]
                min_sum=min(min_sum,s)
            if j+1<n:
                s=cur+labels[(i,j+1)]
                min_sum=min(min_sum,s)
    return min_sum

print(assign_by_degree())

def assign_checkerboard(n=60):
    # assign low numbers to black cells (i+j even)
    low_numbers = list(range(1, n*n//2 + 1))
    high_numbers = list(range(n*n//2 + 1, n*n + 1))
    random.shuffle(low_numbers)
    random.shuffle(high_numbers)
    labels={}
    low_idx=0
    high_idx=0
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                labels[(i,j)]=low_numbers[low_idx]; low_idx+=1
            else:
                labels[(i,j)]=high_numbers[high_idx]; high_idx+=1
    # compute min sum
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            cur=labels[(i,j)]
            if i+1<n:
                s=cur+labels[(i+1,j)]
                min_sum=min(min_sum,s)
            if j+1<n:
                s=cur+labels[(i,j+1)]
                min_sum=min(min_sum,s)
    return min_sum

print(assign_checkerboard())

    low_numbers = list(range(1, n*n//2 + 1))
    high_numbers = list(range(n*n//2 + 1, n*n + 1))
    random.shuffle(low_numbers)
    random.shuffle(high_numbers)
    labels={}
    low_idx=0
    high_idx=0
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                labels[(i,j)]=low_numbers[low_idx]; low_idx+=1
            else:
                labels[(i,j)]=high_numbers[high_idx]; high_idx+=1
    # compute min sum
    min_sum=10**9
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            cur=labels[(i,j)]
            if i+1<n:
                s=cur+labels[(i+1,j)]
                min_sum=min(min_sum,s)
            if j+1<n:
                s=cur+labels[(i,j+1)]
                min_sum=min(min_sum,s)
    return min_sum

Minimal sum 1812 (random ordering). That's close to 1802 lower bound earlier. The minimal sum is determined by the smallest low number (maybe 1) plus the smallest high neighbor among its adjacent white cells. Since we shuffled randomly, the smallest high neighbor may be as low as 1801, giving sum 1802. But we got 1812, maybe due to random assignment.

Thus the maximum S is at most maybe around 1800+something. Let's try to optimize ordering: assign low numbers to black cells in increasing order (so 1 at some black cell), and assign high numbers to white cells also increasing order (so 1801 at some white cell). The minimal sum will be 1 + min adjacent white number. We can try to place the smallest high numbers far from the smallest low numbers. Since each black cell's neighbors are white cells, we can try to assign the smallest high numbers to white cells that are far from black cells with small low numbers (i.e., far away). This is like a matching problem: we want to maximize min_{black a} (a + min_{white neighbor of a} w). Equivalent to maximize minimal sum across edges.

Given we can permute numbers arbitrarily within each color, we can treat this as a bipartite assignment problem: we have black cells each with a set of neighboring white cells (up to 4). We need to assign high numbers to white cells to maximize minimal sum across edges. This is reminiscent of the "bottleneck assignment" problem.

Potentially the optimal arrangement is to assign numbers such that for each black cell, the minimum among its neighboring white numbers is as large as possible, and the overall minimal across black cells is maximized.

Since the grid is regular, we might achieve a uniform distribution: assign high numbers to white cells in decreasing order such that each black cell's neighbors have relatively high numbers, maybe the smallest high number assigned to a white cell that is adjacent only to black cells with relatively large low numbers.

Thus the minimal sum may be around something like 1800 + something.

Let's attempt to compute the optimal value via solving a bottleneck assignment problem using linear programming: we want to maximize S such that there exists labeling with numbers 1..1800 on black cells, 1801..3600 on white cells, and for each edge (b,w), b + w >= S.

Since we can permute numbers within each color arbitrarily, we can treat this as: we have black labels a_i (sorted ascending), white labels b_j (sorted ascending). We need to assign each white label to a white cell, each black label to a black cell, such that for each adjacency edge (i,j) we have a_i + b_j >= S. This is a combinatorial problem.

But perhaps we can achieve S = 1800 + 1801 = 3601? No, because we need each edge sum >= S, and smallest black label is 1, smallest white label is 1801, sum = 1802. So S cannot exceed 1802 if we have that pairing. But we can assign 1 to a black cell whose neighbors are white cells with labels maybe close to 3600, but the smallest white label overall could be placed far away, not adjacent to 1. So S could be larger than 1802.

Thus the maximum S is at most 1 + max possible minimal white neighbor for the black cell with label 1. Since we can place 1 at a corner (degree 2) and assign its two white neighbors to be the two largest numbers 3600 and 3599, giving sum = 1+3599 = 3600 (or 1+3600 = 3601). So the minimal sum could be as high as 3600 if we can arrange such that the smallest black label's neighbors are the largest numbers, and similarly for other low black labels.

Thus S could be close to 3600. The challenge is to assign all low black numbers such that each has high white neighbors, while also ensuring white cells have enough high numbers to serve multiple low black cells.

This is similar to constructing a Latin-type arrangement where high numbers are placed on white cells that are adjacent to many low black cells. Since interior white cells have degree 4, they can serve up to 4 low black cells.

Thus we might be able to achieve S close to 3600.

[CODE] (turn 3):
def max_min_adj_sum(n):
    # n is side length of square grid, total cells n*n
    N=n*n
    best=0
    best_arr=None
    # brute force for small n (n<=3 maybe)
    cells=list(range(N))
    # adjacency list edges (undirected)
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if i+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, (i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1)))
    # iterate permutations (maybe too many)
    from itertools import permutations
    for perm in permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        # compute min edge sum
        mins=math.inf
        for u,v in edges:
            s=perm[u]+perm[v]
            if s<mins:
                mins=s
                if mins<best: # prune
                    break
        else:
            if mins>best:
                best=mins
                best_arr=perm
    return best, best_arr

print(max_min_adj_sum(2))

[CODE] (turn 3):
def max_min_adj_sum(n):
    # n is side length of square grid, total cells n*n
    N=n*n
    best=0
    best_arr=None
    # brute force for small n (n<=3 maybe)
    cells=list(range(N))
    # adjacency list edges (undirected)
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if i+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, (i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1)))
    # iterate permutations (maybe too many)
    from itertools import permutations
    for perm in permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        # compute min edge sum
        mins=math.inf
        for u,v in edges:
            s=perm[u]+perm[v]
            if s<mins:
                mins=s
                if mins<best: # prune
                    break
        else:
            if mins>best:
                best=mins
                best_arr=perm
    return best, best_arr

print(max_min_adj_sum(2))

            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1)))
    best=0
    best_perm=None
    from itertools import permutations
    for perm in permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        mins=math.inf
        for u,v in edges:
            s=perm[u]+perm[v]
            if s<mins:
                mins=s
                if mins<best:
                    break

    return best, best_perm

def min_adj_sum(grid, n):
    mins=math.inf
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            v=grid[idx]
            if i+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[(i+1)*n+j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[i*n+(j+1)])

import itertools, math, sys, time

def max_S_grid(n, time_limit=30):
    N=n*n
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if i+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, (i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1)))
    # adjacency list per cell
    adj=[set() for _ in range(N)]
    for u,v in edges:
        adj[u].add(v)
        adj[v].add(u)
    numbers=list(range(1,N+1))
    best=0
    best_assign=None
    start=time.time()
    # order cells by degree descending for pruning
    order=sorted(range(N), key=lambda x: -len(adj[x]))
    # Precompute neighbor indices in order
    # Use backtracking with domain pruning: for each cell, we try numbers and check constraints with already assigned neighbors.
    assign=[None]*N
    used=[False]*(N+1)
    def backtrack(pos):
        nonlocal best,best_assign
        if time.time()-start>time_limit:
            raise TimeoutError
        if pos==N:
            # compute min edge sum
            mins=math.inf
            for u,v in edges:
                s=assign[u]+assign[v]
                if s<mins:
                    mins=s
            if mins>best:
                best=mins
                best_assign=assign.copy()
            return
        cell=order[pos]
        # try numbers in descending order maybe to get higher min early
        for num in range(N,0,-1):
            if used[num]:
                continue
            # check constraints with already assigned neighbors
            ok=True
            for nb in adj[cell]:
                if assign[nb] is not None:
                    if assign[nb]+num < best: # cannot exceed current best
                        ok=False
                        break
            if not ok:
                continue
            # assign
            assign[cell]=num
            used[num]=True
            backtrack(pos+1)
            assign[cell]=None
            used[num]=False
    try:
        backtrack(0)
    except TimeoutError:
        pass
    return best, best_assign

best4, assign4 = max_S_grid(4, time_limit=10)
best4

import itertools, math, sys, time

def max_S_grid(n, time_limit=30):
    N=n*n
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if i+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, (i+1)*n+j))
            if j+1<n:
                edges.append((idx, i*n+(j+1)))
    # adjacency list per cell
    adj=[set() for _ in range(N)]
    for u,v in edges:
        adj[u].add(v)
        adj[v].add(u)
    numbers=list(range(1,N+1))
    best=0
    best_assign=None
    start=time.time()
    # order cells by degree descending for pruning
    order=sorted(range(N), key=lambda x: -len(adj[x]))
    # Precompute neighbor indices in order
    # Use backtracking with domain pruning: for each cell, we try numbers and check constraints with already assigned neighbors.
    assign=[None]*N
    used=[False]*(N+1)
    def backtrack(pos):
        nonlocal best,best_assign
        if time.time()-start>time_limit:
            raise TimeoutError
        if pos==N:
            # compute min edge sum
            mins=math.inf
            for u,v in edges:
                s=assign[u]+assign[v]
                if s<mins:
                    mins=s
            if mins>best:
                best=mins
                best_assign=assign.copy()
            return
        cell=order[pos]
        # try numbers in descending order maybe to get higher min early
        for num in range(N,0,-1):
            if used[num]:
                continue
            # check constraints with already assigned neighbors
            ok=True
            for nb in adj[cell]:
                if assign[nb] is not None:
                    if assign[nb]+num < best: # cannot exceed current best
                        ok=False
                        break
            if not ok:
                continue
            # assign
            assign[cell]=num
            used[num]=True
            backtrack(pos+1)
            assign[cell]=None
            used[num]=False
    try:
        backtrack(0)
    except TimeoutError:
        pass
    return best, best_assign

[CODE] (turn 11):
def checkerboard_assign(n):
    N=n*n
    # assign smallest to black, largest to white
    numbers=list(range(1,N+1))
    black_nums=numbers[:N//2]  # first half
    white_nums=numbers[N//2:]  # second half
    # fill grid row-major
    grid=[0]*N
    black_iter=iter(black_nums)
    white_iter=iter(white_nums)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                grid[idx]=next(black_iter)
            else:
                grid[idx]=next(white_iter)
    return grid

def min_adj_sum_grid(grid, n):
    mins=math.inf
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            v=grid[idx]
            if i+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[(i+1)*n+j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[i*n+(j+1)])
    return mins

grid4=checkerboard_assign(4)
min_adj_sum_grid(grid4,4)

[CODE] (turn 12):
import random, math, time

def random_grid(n):
    N=n*n
    perm=list(range(1,N+1))
    random.shuffle(perm)
    return perm

def min_adj_sum_grid(grid, n):
    mins=math.inf
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            v=grid[idx]
            if i+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[(i+1)*n+j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[i*n+(j+1)])
    return mins

def improve_grid(grid, n, iterations=100000):
    best=grid[:]
    best_score=min_adj_sum_grid(best,n)
    cur=grid[:]
    cur_score=best_score
    for it in range(iterations):
        # random swap two cells
        i,j=random.sample(range(len(cur)),2)
        cur[i],cur[j]=cur[j],cur[i]
        new_score=min_adj_sum_grid(cur,n)
        if new_score>=cur_score:
            cur_score=new_score
            if new_score>best_score:
                best_score=new_score
                best=cur[:]
        else:
            # revert
            cur[i],cur[j]=cur[j],cur[i]
    return best_score,best

random.seed(0)
grid=checkerboard_assign(4)
score=min_adj_sum_grid(grid,4)
print("initial",score)
best_score,best_grid=improve_grid(grid,4,iterations=20000)
print("best",best_score)

import random, math, time

def random_grid(n):
    N=n*n
    perm=list(range(1,N+1))
    random.shuffle(perm)
    return perm

def min_adj_sum_grid(grid, n):
    mins=math.inf
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            v=grid[idx]
            if i+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[(i+1)*n+j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[i*n+(j+1)])
    return mins

def improve_grid(grid, n, iterations=100000):
    best=grid[:]
    best_score=min_adj_sum_grid(best,n)
    cur=grid[:]
    cur_score=best_score
    for it in range(iterations):
        # random swap two cells
        i,j=random.sample(range(len(cur)),2)
        cur[i],cur[j]=cur[j],cur[i]
        new_score=min_adj_sum_grid(cur,n)
        if new_score>=cur_score:
            cur_score=new_score
            if new_score>best_score:
                best_score=new_score
                best=cur[:]
        else:
            # revert
            cur[i],cur[j]=cur[j],cur[i]
    return best_score,best

random.seed(0)
grid=checkerboard_assign(4)
score=min_adj_sum_grid(grid,4)
print("initial",score)
best_score,best_grid=improve_grid(grid,4,iterations=20000)
print("best",best_score)

[CODE] (turn 14):
def search_best(n, trials=20, iters=20000):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for t in range(trials):
        grid=checkerboard_assign(n)
        score,_=improve_grid(grid,n,iters)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=grid[:]
    return best_score,best_grid

best_score,best_grid=search_best(4,trials=30,iters=30000)

def max_min_sum_heuristic(n, restarts=20, iters=50000):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for r in range(restarts):
        # start with checkerboard assignment
        grid=checkerboard_assign(n)
        # random shuffle a bit
        random.shuffle(grid)
        # improve
        score,_=improve_grid(grid,n,iters)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=grid[:]
    return best_score, best_grid

best5, grid5 = max_min_sum_heuristic(5, restarts=10, iters=40000)

[CODE] (turn 16):
def checkerboard_assign_general(n):
    N=n*n
    # count black cells
    black_cells = sum(1 for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0)
    white_cells = N - black_cells
    numbers=list(range(1,N+1))
    black_nums=numbers[:black_cells]
    white_nums=numbers[black_cells:]
    grid=[0]*N
    bi=0
    wi=0
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                grid[idx]=black_nums[bi]; bi+=1
            else:
                grid[idx]=white_nums[wi]; wi+=1
    return grid

grid5 = checkerboard_assign_general(5)
min_adj_sum_grid(grid5,5)

def checkerboard_assign_general(n):
    N=n*n
    # count black cells
    black_cells = sum(1 for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0)
    white_cells = N - black_cells
    numbers=list(range(1,N+1))
    black_nums=numbers[:black_cells]
    white_nums=numbers[black_cells:]
    grid=[0]*N
    bi=0
    wi=0
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                grid[idx]=black_nums[bi]; bi+=1
            else:
                grid[idx]=white_nums[wi]; wi+=1
    return grid

    best=grid[:]
    best_score=min_adj_sum_grid(best,n)
    cur=grid[:]
    cur_score=best_score
    N=n*n
    for it in range(iterations):
        i,j=random.sample(range(N),2)
        cur[i],cur[j]=cur[j],cur[i]
        new_score=min_adj_sum_grid(cur,n)
        if new_score>=cur_score:
            cur_score=new_score
            if new_score>best_score:
                best_score=new_score
                best=cur[:]
        else:
            cur[i],cur[j]=cur[j],cur[i]
    return best_score,best

def search_best_n(n, restarts=30, iters=150000):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for r in range(restarts):
        grid=checkerboard_assign_general(n)
        random.shuffle(grid)
        score,_=improve_grid_general(grid,n,iters)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=grid[:]
    return best_score,best_grid

[CODE] (turn 21):
def min_adj_sum_grid_with_edges(grid,n):
    mins=math.inf
    min_edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            v=grid[idx]
            if i+1<n:
                s=v+grid[(i+1)*n+j]
                if s<mins:
                    mins=s
                    min_edges=[((i,j),(i+1,j))]
                elif s==mins:
                    min_edges.append(((i,j),(i+1,j)))
            if j+1<n:
                s=v+grid[i*n+(j+1)]
                if s<mins:
                    mins=s
                    min_edges=[((i,j),(i,j+1))]
                elif s==mins:
                    min_edges.append(((i,j),(i,j+1)))
    return mins, min_edges

def find_best_grid(n, restarts=20, iters=200000):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for r in range(restarts):
        grid=checkerboard_assign_general(n)
        random.shuffle(grid)
        score,grid2=improve_grid_general(grid,n,iters)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=grid2[:]
    return best_score,best_grid

import random, math, time

def min_adj_sum_grid(grid,n):
    mins=math.inf
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            v=grid[idx]
            if i+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[(i+1)*n+j])
            if j+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[i*n+(j+1)])
    return mins

def checkerboard_assign_general(n):
    N=n*n
    black_cells = sum(1 for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0)
    numbers=list(range(1,N+1))
    black_nums=numbers[:black_cells]
    white_nums=numbers[black_cells:]
    grid=[0]*N
    bi=0; wi=0
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx=i*n+j
            if (i+j)%2==0:
                grid[idx]=black_nums[bi]; bi+=1
            else:
                grid[idx]=white_nums[wi]; wi+=1
    return grid

def improve_grid(grid,n,iterations=50000):
    best=grid[:]
    best_score=min_adj_sum_grid(best,n)
    cur=grid[:]
    cur_score=best_score
    N=n*n
    for _ in range(iterations):
        i,j=random.sample(range(N),2)
        cur[i],cur[j]=cur[j],cur[i]
        new_score=min_adj_sum_grid(cur,n)
        if new_score>=cur_score:
            cur_score=new_score
            if new_score>best_score:
                best_score=new_score
                best=cur[:]
        else:
            cur[i],cur[j]=cur[j],cur[i]
    return best_score,best

def search_best(n,restarts=10,iters=60000):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for r in range(restarts):
        grid=checkerboard_assign_general(n)
        random.shuffle(grid)
        score,grid2=improve_grid(grid,n,iters)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=grid2[:]
    return best_score,best_grid

best5,grid5_best=search_best(5,restarts=12,iters=80000)
best5

def min_adj_sum_grid(grid,n):
    mins=math.inf
    for i in range(n):

            v=grid[idx]
            if i+1<n:
                mins=min(mins, v+grid[(i+1)*n+j])
            if j+1<n:

    return mins

def checkerboard_assign_general(n):
    N=n*n
    black_cells = sum(1 for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if (i+j)%2==0)
    numbers=list(range(1,N+1))
    black_nums=numbers[:black_cells]
    white_nums=numbers[black_cells:]
    grid=[0]*N

            if (i+j)%2==0:
                grid[idx]=black_nums[bi]; bi+=1
            else:
                grid[idx]=white_nums[wi]; wi+=1
    return grid

def improve_grid(grid,n,iterations=50000):
    best=grid[:]
    best_score=min_adj_sum_grid(best,n)
    cur=grid[:]
    cur_score=best_score
    N=n*n
    for _ in range(iterations):

        else:

    return best_score,best

def search_best(n,restarts=10,iters=60000):
    best_score=0
    best_grid=None
    for r in range(restarts):
        grid=checkerboard_assign_general(n)
        random.shuffle(grid)
        score,grid2=improve_grid(grid,n,iters)
        if score>best_score:
            best_score=score
            best_grid=grid2[:]
    return best_score,best_grid

[CODE] (turn 32):
def try_target_S(n, target, max_iters=200000):
    N=n*n
    # start with random permutation
    grid=list(range(1,N+1))
    random.shuffle(grid)
    cur_score=min_adj_sum_grid(grid,n)
    # if already >= target, success
    if cur_score>=target:
        return True, grid, cur_score
    for it in range(max_iters):
        i,j=random.sample(range(N),2)
        # swap
        grid[i],grid[j]=grid[j],grid[i]
        new_score=min_adj_sum_grid(grid,n)
        # accept if new_score >= cur_score or maybe if new_score >= target
        if new_score >= cur_score:
            cur_score=new_score
            if cur_score>=target:
                return True, grid, cur_score
        else:
            # revert
            grid[i],grid[j]=grid[j],grid[i]
    return False, grid, cur_score

found,grid6,score=try_target_S(6,35, max_iters=500000)
found, score

def try_target_S(n, target, max_iters=200000):
    N=n*n
    # start with random permutation
    grid=list(range(1,N+1))
    random.shuffle(grid)
    cur_score=min_adj_sum_grid(grid,n)
    # if already >= target, success
    if cur_score>=target:
        return True, grid, cur_score
    for it in range(max_iters):
        i,j=random.sample(range(N),2)
        # swap
        grid[i],grid[j]=grid[j],grid[i]
        new_score=min_adj_sum_grid(grid,n)
        # accept if new_score >= cur_score or maybe if new_score >= target
        if new_score >= cur_score:
            cur_score=new_score
            if cur_score>=target:
                return True, grid, cur_score
        else:
            # revert
            grid[i],grid[j]=grid[j],grid[i]
    return False, grid, cur_score

[REASONING] (turn 33):
We didn't reach 35, got 33. Could be max is 33 for n=6? Let's compute theoretical bound.

Consider the smallest number a = 1. Its degree d <= 4. Its neighbors must be >= S - 1. So we need at least d distinct numbers >= S - 1. Since there are only N - (S-2) numbers >= S-1? Actually numbers >= S-1 are from S-1 up to N. Count = N - (S-2). For S large, this count may be insufficient to provide distinct neighbors for many low numbers.

We can attempt to derive bound using counting of edges incident to low numbers.

Let’s sort numbers ascending: 1,2,...,N. For each k, consider vertices with value <= k. They can only be adjacent to vertices with value >= S - k. So edges between two low vertices (both <= k) are forbidden unless both satisfy sum >= S, which would require each pair sum >= S, i.e., for any two low numbers a,b <= k, we need a+b >= S. The worst case is a=1,b=1? Not allowed because distinct numbers. So to avoid low-low adjacency, we need that the set of low numbers is such that any two of them sum >= S. That means the smallest two numbers among low set must sum >= S. So if we include numbers up to t, then 1 + t >= S => t >= S-1. So the low set can include numbers up to at most S-1 (since 1 + (S-1) = S). But if we include numbers up to S-1, then 2 + (S-2) also = S, etc. So any pair of numbers whose sum < S must not be adjacent.

Thus we can think of constructing a graph where edges are forbidden between numbers whose sum < S. We need to embed the numbers onto grid such that no forbidden edge occurs.

Thus the problem reduces to: Find largest S such that the graph G_S (grid) can be labeled with numbers 1..N where any edge connects numbers whose sum >= S.

Equivalently, we need a graph homomorphism from grid to complement of "sum < S" relation.

This is similar to "bandwidth" problem.

We can attempt to find upper bound via counting of edges that must be between low numbers.

Consider the set L = {1,2,...,S-1}. Any two numbers in L sum <= 2(S-1). Actually the maximum sum of two numbers in L is (S-1)+(S-2) = 2S-3 which may be >= S. So not all pairs in L are forbidden. Only pairs where sum < S are forbidden. For numbers a,b in L, sum < S iff a + b <= S-1. So the forbidden pairs are those where a + b <= S-1.

Thus the graph of forbidden pairs among low numbers is like a threshold graph: pairs of small numbers that sum less than S.

We need to embed numbers such that no edge connects a forbidden pair.

Thus we need to find S such that the grid graph can avoid these forbidden pairs.

This is similar to edge coloring with constraints.

Perhaps we can compute maximum S via greedy algorithm: assign numbers to cells in decreasing order, ensuring each placed number does not create a low sum with already placed neighbors.

We can simulate this for n=60 to estimate maximum S. Use algorithm: order cells by degree descending (interior first) maybe, assign numbers from largest to smallest, ensuring each edge sum >= S (unknown). We can binary search S and test feasibility using greedy/backtracking.

But 60x60 = 3600 cells, backtracking impossible. However we can use constructive pattern to achieve S = N - 1 = 3599? Let's

[CODE] (turn 2):
def max_min_adj_sum(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    # adjacency edges list of pairs of indices
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj): # avoid double
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    best=-1
    best_perm=None
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        min_sum=math.inf
        for u,v in edges:
            s=perm[u]+perm[v]
            if s<min_sum:
                min_sum=s
                if min_sum<=best: # prune
                    break
        else:
            if min_sum>best:
                best=min_sum
                best_perm=perm
    return best, best_perm

print(max_min_adj_sum(2,2))

def max_min_adj_sum(m,n):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    # adjacency edges list of pairs of indices
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj): # avoid double
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    best=-1
    best_perm=None
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        min_sum=math.inf
        for u,v in edges:
            s=perm[u]+perm[v]
            if s<min_sum:
                min_sum=s
                if min_sum<=best: # prune
                    break
        else:
            if min_sum>best:
                best=min_sum
                best_perm=perm
    return best, best_perm

def min_adj_sum(m,n,perm):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj):
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    return min(perm[u]+perm[v] for u,v in edges)

def max_min_adj_sum_path(n):
    N=n
    edges=[(i,i+1) for i in range(N-1)]
    best=-1
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        min_sum=min(perm[i]+perm[i+1] for i in range(N-1))
        if min_sum>best:
            best=min_sum
    return best

def exists_S(m,n,S):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj):
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        if all(perm[u]+perm[v]>=S for u,v in edges):
            return True, perm
    return False, None

print(exists_S(2,2,5)[0])

def exists_S(m,n,S):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj):
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(1,N+1)):
        if all(perm[u]+perm[v]>=S for u,v in edges):
            return True, perm
    return False, None

[CODE] (turn 18):
def max_min_adj_sum_sa(m,n,iterations=200000):
    N=m*n
    # adjacency list
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    for i,j in cells:
        u=idx[(i,j)]
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                v=idx[(ni,nj)]
                neighbors[u].append(v)
    # start random permutation
    perm = list(range(1,N+1))
    random.shuffle(perm)
    def min_adj(perm):
        min_sum=10**9
        for u in range(N):
            pu=perm[u]
            for v in neighbors[u]:
                if u<v: # count each edge once
                    s=pu+perm[v]
                    if s<min_sum:
                        min_sum=s
        return min_sum
    best=min_adj(perm)
    best_perm=perm[:]
    for it in range(iterations):
        # random swap
        i,j=random.sample(range(N),2)
        perm[i],perm[j]=perm[j],perm[i]
        cur=min_adj(perm)
        if cur>best:
            best=cur
            best_perm=perm[:]
            #print("new best",best,it)
        # accept if not worse? simple hill climbing: keep if cur>=best? Actually we want to explore.
        # We'll accept if cur>=best or with some probability.
        if cur<best:
            # revert with probability maybe
            # simple simulated annealing: accept with probability exp((cur-best)/T)
            # but we don't have T schedule; just revert
            perm[i],perm[j]=perm[j],perm[i]
    return best,best_perm

best, perm = max_min_adj_sum_sa(3,4,iterations=20000)
best

[CODE] (turn 18):
def max_min_adj_sum_sa(m,n,iterations=200000):
    N=m*n
    # adjacency list
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    neighbors = [[] for _ in range(N)]
    for i,j in cells:
        u=idx[(i,j)]
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:

[CODE] (turn 21):
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds, OptimizeResult
import numpy as np, math, itertools, sys, time, collections

[OUTPUT] (turn 21):
[WARN] No output. Use print() to see results.

[CODE] (turn 22):
def max_S_grid(m,n, time_limit=30):
    N=m*n
    # cell indices
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    # edges
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj):
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    E=len(edges)
    # variable ordering: v (N), x (N*N), S (1)
    var_N = N + N*N + 1
    # Build constraints
    # We'll use LinearConstraint with A matrix dense? Use scipy.sparse for efficiency.
    import scipy.sparse as sp
    rows=[]
    cols=[]
    data=[]
    lb=[]
    ub=[]
    # 1) cell assignment sum_k x_{i,k} = 1
    for i in range(N):
        for k in range(N):
            var_index = N + i*N + k  # x_{i,k}
            rows.append(len(lb))
            cols.append(var_index)
            data.append(1)
        lb.append(1)
        ub.append(1)
    # 2) number assignment sum_i x_{i,k} = 1
    for k in range(N):
        for i in range(N):
            var_index = N + i*N + k
            rows.append(len(lb))
            cols.append(var_index)
            data.append(1)
        lb.append(1)
        ub.append(1)
    # 3) definition v_i - sum_k k+1? careful numbers start at 1
    for i in range(N):
        # v_i coefficient
        rows.append(len(lb))
        cols.append(i)  # v_i
        data.append(1)
        # subtract k * x_{i,k}
        for k in range(N):
            var_index = N + i*N + k
            rows.append(len(lb))
            cols.append(var_index)
            data.append(- (k+1))  # number value
        lb.append(0)
        ub.append(0)
    # 4) edge constraints v_i + v_j - S >= 0  => v_i + v_j - S >= 0
    # We'll set lower bound 0, upper bound +inf
    for (i,j) in edges:
        rows.append(len(lb))
        cols.append(i)  # v_i
        data.append(1)
        rows.append(len(lb))
        cols.append(j)  # v_j
        data.append(1)
        # S variable index = N + N*N
        S_idx = N + N*N
        rows.append(len(lb))
        cols.append(S_idx)
        data.append(-1)
        lb.append(0)
        ub.append(np.inf)
    # Build sparse matrix
    A = sp.csr_matrix((data, (np.arange(len(data)), cols)), shape=(len(lb), var_N))
    # variable bounds
    lower_bounds = np.zeros(var_N)
    upper_bounds = np.full(var_N, np.inf)
    # v_i bounds: 1..N maybe
    lower_bounds[:N] = 1
    upper_bounds[:N] = N
    # x binary bounds 0..1
    lower_bounds[N:N+N*N] = 0
    upper_bounds[N:N+N*N] = 1
    # S bounds: 0..2N maybe
    lower_bounds[-1] = 0
    upper_bounds[-1] = 2*N
    # integrality: all variables integer (including v and S)
    integrality = np.ones(var_N, dtype=int)
    # objective: maximize S (last variable)
    c = np.zeros(var_N)
    c[-1] = -1  # milp minimizes c^T x, so negative to maximize
    # Solve
    res = milp(c=c, A_eq=None, b_eq=None, bounds=Bounds(lower_bounds, upper_bounds),
               constraints=LinearConstraint(A, lb, ub), integrality=integrality,
               options={'disp': False, 'time_limit': time_limit})
    return res

res = max_S_grid(2,2)
print(res.success, res.x[-1])

[CODE] (turn 23):
def max_S_grid(m,n, time_limit=30):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj):
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    E=len(edges)
    var_N = N + N*N + 1
    import scipy.sparse as sp
    row_idx=[]
    col_idx=[]
    data=[]
    lb=[]
    ub=[]
    # 1) cell assignment
    for i in range(N):
        for k in range(N):
            row = len(lb)
            row_idx.append(row)
            col_idx.append(N + i*N + k)
            data.append(1)
        lb.append(1)
        ub.append(1)
    # 2) number assignment
    for k in range(N):
        for i in range(N):
            row = len(lb)
            row_idx.append(row)
            col_idx.append(N + i*N + k)
            data.append(1)
        lb.append(1)
        ub.append(1)
    # 3) definition v_i - sum k*x =0
    for i in range(N):
        row = len(lb)
        # v_i coefficient
        row_idx.append(row)
        col_idx.append(i)
        data.append(1)
        # subtract k*x
        for k in range(N):
            row_idx.append(row)
            col_idx.append(N + i*N + k)
            data.append(-(k+1))
        lb.append(0)
        ub.append(0)
    # 4) edge constraints v_i + v_j - S >=0
    S_idx = N + N*N
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row = len(lb)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(i); data.append(1)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(j); data.append(1)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(S_idx); data.append(-1)
        lb.append(0)
        ub.append(np.inf)
    A = sp.csr_matrix((data, (row_idx, col_idx)), shape=(len(lb), var_N))
    # bounds
    lower_bounds = np.zeros(var_N)
    upper_bounds = np.full(var_N, np.inf)
    lower_bounds[:N] = 1
    upper_bounds[:N] = N
    lower_bounds[N:N+N*N] = 0
    upper_bounds[N:N+N*N] = 1
    lower_bounds[-1] = 0
    upper_bounds[-1] = 2*N
    integrality = np.ones(var_N, dtype=int)
    c = np.zeros(var_N)
    c[-1] = -1
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=LinearConstraint(A, lb, ub), bounds=Bounds(lower_bounds, upper_bounds),
               integrality=integrality, options={'disp': False, 'time_limit': time_limit})
    return res

res = max_S_grid(2,2, time_limit=10)
print(res.success, res.x[-1])

        lb.append(0)
        ub.append(np.inf)
    A = sp.csr_matrix((data, (row_idx, col_idx)), shape=(len(lb), var_N))

    lower_bounds = np.zeros(var_N)
    upper_bounds = np.full(var_N, np.inf)
    lower_bounds[:N] = 1
    upper_bounds[:N] = N
    lower_bounds[N:N+N*N] = 0
    upper_bounds[N:N+N*N] = 1
    lower_bounds[-1] = 0
    upper_bounds[-1] = 2*N
    integrality = np.ones(var_N, dtype=int)
    c = np.zeros(var_N)
    c[-1] = -1
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=LinearConstraint(A, lb, ub), bounds=Bounds(lower_bounds, upper_bounds),
               integrality=integrality, options={'disp': False, 'time_limit': time_limit})
    return res

[CODE] (turn 28):
def max_S_grid(m,n, time_limit=30):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj):
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    var_N = N + N*N + 1
    import scipy.sparse as sp
    row_idx=[]
    col_idx=[]
    data=[]
    lb=[]
    ub=[]
    # cell constraints
    for i in range(N):
        for k in range(N):
            row=len(lb)
            row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(N + i*N + k); data.append(1)
        lb.append(1); ub.append(1)
    # number constraints
    for k in range(N):
        for i in range(N):
            row=len(lb)
            row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(N + i*N + k); data.append(1)
        lb.append(1); ub.append(1)
    # definition constraints
    for i in range(N):
        row=len(lb)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(i); data.append(1)
        for k in range(N):
            row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(N + i*N + k); data.append(-(k+1))
        lb.append(0); ub.append(0)
    # edge constraints
    S_idx = N + N*N
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row=len(lb)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(i); data.append(1)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(j); data.append(1)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(S_idx); data.append(-1)
        lb.append(0); ub.append(np.inf)
    A = sp.csr_matrix((data, (row_idx, col_idx)), shape=(len(lb), var_N))
    # bounds
    lower_bounds = np.zeros(var_N)
    upper_bounds = np.full(var_N, np.inf)
    lower_bounds[:N] = 1
    upper_bounds[:N] = N
    lower_bounds[N:N+N*N] = 0
    upper_bounds[N:N+N*N] = 1
    lower_bounds[-1] = 0
    upper_bounds[-1] = 2*N
    integrality = np.ones(var_N, dtype=int)
    c = np.zeros(var_N)
    c[-1] = -1
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=LinearConstraint(A, lb, ub), bounds=Bounds(lower_bounds, upper_bounds),
               integrality=integrality, options={'disp': False, 'time_limit': time_limit})
    return res

    N=m*n

    var_N = N + N*N + 1

    col_idx=[]
    data=[]
    lb=[]
    ub=[]

    # number constraints

            row=len(lb)
            row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(N + i*N + k); data.append(1)
        lb.append(1); ub.append(1)
    # definition constraints

        for k in range(N):
            row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(N + i*N + k); data.append(-(k+1))
        lb.append(0); ub.append(0)
    # edge constraints

        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(j); data.append(1)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(S_idx); data.append(-1)

def max_S_grid(m,n, time_limit=30):
    N=m*n
    cells = [(i,j) for i in range(m) for j in range(n)]
    idx = {cells[i]:i for i in range(N)}
    edges=[]
    for i,j in cells:
        for di,dj in [(1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)]:
            ni,nj=i+di,j+dj
            if 0<=ni<m and 0<=nj<n:
                if (i,j) < (ni,nj):
                    edges.append((idx[(i,j)], idx[(ni,nj)]))
    var_N = N + N*N + 1
    import scipy.sparse as sp
    row_idx=[]; col_idx=[]; data=[]; lb=[]; ub=[]
    # cell constraints
    for i in range(N):
        for k in range(N):
            row=len(lb)
            row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(N + i*N + k); data.append(1)
        lb.append(1); ub.append(1)
    # number constraints
    for k in range(N):
        for i in range(N):
            row=len(lb)
            row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(N + i*N + k); data.append(1)
        lb.append(1); ub.append(1)
    # definition constraints
    for i in range(N):
        row=len(lb)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(i); data.append(1)
        for k in range(N):
            row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(N + i*N + k); data.append(-(k+1))
        lb.append(0); ub.append(0)
    # edge constraints
    S_idx = N + N*N
    for (i,j) in edges:
        row=len(lb)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(i); data.append(1)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(j); data.append(1)
        row_idx.append(row); col_idx.append(S_idx); data.append(-1)
        lb.append(0); ub.append(np.inf)
    A = sp.csr_matrix((data, (row_idx, col_idx)), shape=(len(lb), var_N))

[REASONING] (turn 39):
No violations, meaning the necessary condition holds for S=N+1? Wait we used black degrees sorted ascending vs white degrees descending. Since both sets have same multiset of degrees (2 corners, 116 degree 3, rest degree 4). Actually black and white have same distribution (since grid symmetric). So cumulative sums will be equal for each k (since both sorted lists same). Indeed both have same degree distribution, so condition holds trivially. So not a contradiction.

Thus the Hall condition does not rule out S=N+1.

But our computational search for small grids shows S=N is max, not N+1. So there must be another obstruction.

Maybe due to parity of sum of numbers? Let's compute total sum of numbers on black cells vs white cells. For any labeling, sum over black cells + sum over white cells = total sum. If we require each edge sum >= N+1, then for each black cell b with value a, each neighbor white cell w must satisfy a + w >= N+1. Summing over all edges incident to a black cell (degree d), we get d*a + sum_{neighbors} w >= d*(N+1). Summing over all black cells, total sum_{black} d*a + sum over each edge counted once from black side of neighbor values >= sum_{black} d*(N+1) = E*(N+1). But sum_{black} d*a = sum_{black} deg(b)*value(b). Similarly sum_{edges} neighbor white values = sum_{edges} value(white). So total left side = sum_{black} deg(b)*value(b) + sum_{edges} value(white). But note sum_{edges} value(white) = sum_{white} deg(w)*value(w) (since each edge counted from white side as well). So total left side = total edge sum = sum_{all vertices} deg(v)*value(v). So we

[REASONING] (turn 1):
We still not seeing output. Possibly the tool integration is not functioning in this conversation; maybe we cannot run code. However we need to compute numbers; we can do mental arithmetic.

But we might need to compute maximum S analytically without brute force.

Let's think more.

Given bipartite nature, the problem reduces to: we have two sets B (black) and W (white) each of size N=1800. We need to assign numbers 1..3600 to B∪W. Goal: maximize minimal sum across edges, where each edge connects a black cell to a white cell that are adjacent in grid.

We can think of constructing a "threshold" S. For a given S, we ask: can we label such that for each edge, b + w >= S? Equivalent to: for each black cell, all adjacent whites must have value >= S - b. Since each black cell has up to 4 adjacent whites (2 for corners, 3 for edge, 4 interior). So we need to assign whites with sufficiently high values to meet constraints.

Thus this is reminiscent of a "graph labeling with lower bounds" problem, which can be solved via Hall's marriage theorem perhaps: treat each black cell as requiring a set of whites with values at least certain threshold.

But because each white can serve multiple black cells (adjacent to up to 4 blacks), the constraints are not simple matching.
